system-proof-benchmarks · base64-v4 · Verus

base64-v4 证明审阅：Argus A 组与 C 组

两组都用 TypeScript 版 Argus 加 system_proof 技能包，后端是 codex，模型是 gpt-6.1-sol，用 Verus 证明 base64 crate 的 STANDARD 编解码往返。本页把两组的结论、规约、证明、Verus 补丁、源码差异和重放日志全文列出，请评审。原始记录（Argus 的每封信、每条命令）在 lbx154/system-proof-base64-v4-results。

结论请重点审C 组材料A 组材料过程与不公平之处复现

结论

C 组
172 verified, 0 errors

插件的原地审计重放 verus-run verify <verification> --preset standard 通过。我在另一个 git worktree 上独立重跑，结果相同。证明对象是真实的 crate 源码，生产代码一个字节没改。

5 小时 55 分钟 · $104.12 · 82 个任务 · 同时最多 13 个

A 组
原地只验证了 1 项

同一条审计重放在原地只验证 1 项：A 的证明代码放在各个 handoff 目录里，靠一份抽出来的副本引进来（如 src/../t13-handoff/chunk-lemmas.rs），验证 checkout 的 src 里只有规约和调用方。这份副本在 A 打过补丁的 Verus 上是 146/0；换原版 Verus 编译不过。

3 小时 06 分钟 · $18.69 · 19 个任务 · 基本串行

逐项对比

插件原地审计重放不通过 1 verified通过 172 verified, 0 errors，独立复核相同

Verus 改动3 个补丁：t9 rust_to_vir_func.rs、t14 syntax.rs 和 sst_to_air.rs；另往 vstd 加了规约，原版 Verus 编译不过2 个编译器补丁：T3、T29；另有一处 vstd 增补（22 行，一条 axiom）。都单独存档并列入可信基

花费 / 用时$18.69 / 3h06$104.12 / 5h55
人工介入无3 次，原文见下文“操作员消息”

请重点审

  1. C 组的两个 Verus 编译器补丁（风险最大）
    • T3：+34/−20 行，把常量初始化的求值推迟到 THIR 擦除初始化之后，并且照样检查所有未用到的常量。
    • T29：+73/−22 行，改了 Rust 函数翻译成 VIR 的 rust_to_vir_func.rs，修 closed-array 签名的匹配。最可能影响可靠性。
    • 证明成立的前提是这两个补丁是对的，它们还没经过 Verus 专家审查。
  2. C 组的 vstd 增补：只加了一条 broadcast axiom，内容是 <[T; N]>::try_from(&[T]) 成功当且仅当 len == N，成功时内容相同。我核对过，和 Rust 标准库的行为一致。
  3. C 组验证副本里的 11 处行为等价改写（C1–C11）：包括把 trait 默认方法里形成字典循环的调用就地展开，因为 Verus 处理不了这种循环。改写和生产代码的对应靠 Reviewer 的论证（TCB-11/12），不是机器证明。完整差异见下面的 verification-vs-production.patch。
  4. C 组的规约是否足够：round_trip_spec.rs 按 RFC 4648 STANDARD 逐字节定义了编码；调用方 caller-t31.rs 证明编码输出逐字节等于这个编码，只用返回的前缀解码就能还原原字节，两个缓冲区后面的部分保持不变。前提：用户的 AsRef/Drop 正常返回，n ≤ 3⌊isize::MAX/4⌋，只在 x86_64 上重放过。
  5. A 组的三个 Verus 补丁，以及它的证明为什么不在原地的源码里。

我核对过的事实：C 组源码里 assume 0 处，admit 0 处，external_body 8 处。8 处里 5 处是这条往返路径用不到的 String/Vec 便利接口（TCB-15），3 处是 be32/be64（TCB-08）和“合法切片长度不超过 isize::MAX”（TCB-04）。佐证：RFC 4648 第 10 节的 7 个测试向量和 771 个运行对比全部通过（报告 METRIC-05）。

C 组材料（全文）

# STANDARD round-trip Audit report

## Result at a glance

| Item | Accepted result |
| --- | --- |
| Verdict | Conditional proof accepted for all three Audit items; report installation/hash confirmation and the required Audit stage check remain completion steps. |
| Original goal | Preserve arbitrary binary bytes through real encode_slice, its reported prefix and decode_slice, with exact lengths, unchanged input and both suffix frames. |
| Production revision | 47fa113a5c309fd7dcf5618d0f738cc9056d4402, unchanged and clean. |
| Active implementation | Original production via the operator-authorized behavior-preserving verification adaptation route; no production repair. |
| Named TOP | TOP-01 Engine::encode_slice; TOP-02 Engine::decode_slice; TOP-03 encoded_len; TOP-04 decoded_len_estimate. |
| Checked caller | Verification-only source-grounded t31 caller; generic event clients and the separate concrete T74 specialization also checked. |
| Independent result | Full installed src/lib.rs: 172 verified, 0 errors, exit 0. Counts from separate runs are not added. |
| Bugs and repairs | No confirmed production codec bug in the accepted scope. Specification/event and compiler integration repairs remain explicit. |
| Trust ledger | 14 direct entries and one residual entry; grouped inherited library/semantic trust is explicitly declared. |
| Lifecycle | Generic conversion and complete owned cleanup must return normally; selected codec continuation has checked finite progress and no permitted codec error/panic. |

## Original goal and final claim

The authoritative [objective](effective-objective.txt) requests lossless STANDARD slice encoding/decoding, not a complete RFC or malformed-input audit. The selected configuration disables default features, enables std with its implied alloc, and uses no SIMD. The independently replayed platform is linux_x86_64.

Let b be the immutable byte slice actually returned by one invocation's single AsRef conversion. For 0 <= n = |b| <= 3 floor(isize::MAX/4), sufficient existing destinations and normal external conversion/cleanup, encoding returns Ok with E(n) = 4 ceil(n/3) canonical bytes. Decoding exactly that reported prefix with D(E(n)) capacity, where D(m) = 3 ceil(m/4), returns Ok with n bytes equal to b. Original destination bytes cannot influence those prefixes, and both destination suffixes remain unchanged. Empty, zero, non-UTF-8 and all block/residue boundary inputs are included. Internal errors, assertion/unwrap failure and codec panic are not extra permitted outcomes.

The bound follows from valid byte-slice representation and padded destination capacity, not whichever values the codec accepts. Its 64-bit maximum is 6,917,529,027,641,081,853 bytes. The formula gives 1,610,612,733 for 32-bit representation; this Audit did not independently replay a 32-bit target. Maximum allocation existence is not promised. Public encoded_len has broader integer Option semantics; integer representability alone does not imply a physically obtainable destination.

The public [open meaning](../src/round_trip_spec.rs#L6-L98) is reply-uniform within each invocation. An earlier preview, timeless admitted-reply relation or arbitrary other callback reply is not substituted for b. Changing callbacks may choose different b in production and adapted builds; the reviewed transfer establishes the same per-event guarantee for each lawful b separately. T74 does not narrow that generic theorem. Safe input preservation concerns the consumed bytes during the codec interval, not preservation of a consumed owned object's representation after arbitrary user Drop.

GeneralPurpose::encode_slice and GeneralPurpose::decode_slice inherit these selected Engine default methods; they are not separate override bodies. STANDARD's actual constructors, alphabet, encoding/inverse tables and padding/settings establish the concrete engine predicates consumed by those contracts.

## Verified usage and proof closure

The [common verifier root](../src/lib.rs#L291-L362) registers the actual targets, supporting proof modules and all selected callers together. Product cfg values select std/alloc; no proof-mode switch changes contracts or resources. The full run has no verify-only selector. Support proofs are consumed by actual body assertions and contracts, rather than replacing the codec.

| Material edge | Audit disposition |
| --- | --- |
| Caller -> public sizing -> physical capacities | Checked sizing bodies and arithmetic lemmas; valid-byte-slice bound is TCB-04. Caller derives sufficient lengths from actual sizing values and supplied existing slices. |
| STANDARD -> alphabet/config/table constructors | Checked concrete project bodies/witnesses establish symbol61, canonical encoding table, inverse decoding table and decoder readiness. |
| Generic public encode -> one conversion -> lexical wrapper/padding continuation | Checked actual annotated wrapper; captured event follows the conversion immediately. TCB-02/05/11 cover erasure, library observation and original-source transfer. |
| Encoder continuation -> internal_encode -> scalar groups/tail -> read_u64/add_padding | Checked project bodies establish exact count, byte content and full frame; TCB-07 covers only standard conversion. Zero-prefix callback executes once and changes no bytes. |
| Returned written count -> &encoded[..written] -> public decode | Checked t31 caller uses only the encoder's prefix, derives canonical image and decoded capacity, unwraps mandatory success and asserts byte equality/input preservation/both frames. |
| Public estimate -> GeneralPurposeEstimate::new -> forwarding | Checked actual sizing chain; TCB-09 covers bool conversion only. |
| Public decode -> actual event -> internal_decode -> boundary selection | Checked actual wrappers/forwarding and canonical-image boundary facts. Capacity is not assumed to excuse an error. |
| Decoder -> complete quads -> chunk4/chunk8 | Checked loop composition, byte-group recovery and complete destination frames; TCB-08 covers primitive endian output only. |
| Decoder -> suffix -> terminal bits/padding -> metadata/map/From | Checked actual adapted suffix plus consumed canonical-terminal/bit facts; metadata projection and actual From<DecodeError> body preserve variants/payloads. No remaining P-T25 or P-READ-U64 effect assumption. Library Result/closure forwarding is TCB-03. |
| Generic changing/variable-prefix clients | Checked event consumers derive obtainable bounds from actual source lengths/envelopes. Their sufficient envelopes do not become public restrictions. Opaque state interfaces provide no prediction of the next event. |
| Prepared producer -> actual canonical event -> decoder | Checked [T74 direct specialization](t74/specialized.rs#L6-L82), with real PointsTo and four observations; reviewed concrete correspondence is TCB-12. No universal Release/Reclaim or arbitrary callback-transport theorem is asserted. |
| Owned conversion/cleanup -> delivery and frames | TCB-13 normal-return and safe-reference separation composition; borrowed t31 inputs and concrete T74 fields have no custom Drop. |
| Convenience/streaming/SIMD/other engines | Out of scope; no unverified body from these paths discharges a selected edge. |

The detailed [call graph and boundary disposition](../t80-handoff/CALL-GRAPH.md) and [located inventory](../t80-handoff/replay/boundaries.json) are reviewed indices, not verification oracles. No active project assume/admit, broadcast axiom declaration or uninterpreted TOP function occurs in the selected sources. Opaque/closed specifications have definitions; empty proof bodies are verifier-checked obligations, not admissions. Imported axiomatic/library contracts remain TCB-03, including the array conversion axiom used by checked read_u64.

## Source identity and correspondence

[Independent before](../t80-handoff/independent-review/before.json) and [after](../t80-handoff/independent-review/after.json) identify all 38 selected inputs, six compiled verifier/library artifacts and three macro inputs. The source registration includes seven path-based caller files; all actual target/support source and relevant imported vstd/builtin artifacts are covered. Product-excluded tests/SIMD source is also pinned. Remaining Rust/core/std runtime semantics and toolchain behavior are declared trust, not silently unhashed project dependencies.

The [production identity](../t80-handoff/production-identity.json) and [after check](../t80-handoff/production-identity-after.json) preserve every tracked production byte and the fixed revision. T80 made evidence changes only. The [raw source diff](../t80-handoff/source.diff), [C1-C11 argument](../t80-handoff/ADAPTATIONS.md#L1-L86), accepted target lineage and actual inspected bodies classify executable expansions/counting-loop/endian/suffix rewrites as operator-authorized Verus adaptations. No new product algorithm or repair is claimed.

The independent [code-drift result](../t80-handoff/independent-review/code-drift.json) returned exit2 because src/encode.rs:190 could not be parsed by its restricted projection. It is incomplete, not a clean correspondence certificate. Direct inspection compared the unchanged padding range/write/return to production. The other located differences were assessed against the explicit adaptations and stable accepted source lineage. Token equality and finite comparisons support, but do not replace, the reviewed semantic continuation argument.

A defect in a direct trusted proposition may invalidate the conclusion. Codec success counts do not prove the compiler or foundational library models.

It selects the same source/std/alloc/rlimit20/pinned route with normal checks, omitting only spinoff-all. Its broad Git fingerprint changed as evidence/untracked files changed; source_stable=false and [unstable-source observation](../t80-handoff/standardized-observation.json) remain verbatim. Exact selected source/library/macro closure is independently stable. Therefore the completed172/0 result supports this pinned candidate without asserting broad repository stability. Supplemental [runner validation behavior](../t80-handoff/runner-validation-excerpt.txt) is not Audit acceptance authority.

Successful installed API runtime checks are not prepared-client comparisons.

## Findings, bugs and repairs

No confirmed in-scope production codec counterexample was found. The prior timeless generic-domain defect was repaired by invocation-specific event capture; current contracts do not assume purity or a canonical next observation. Unsupported compiler constant/trait/endian integration and iterator/continue modeling prompted explicitly reviewed compiler/source adaptations. Abandoned dictionary/ownership simulation investigations supply no theorem and are not hidden prerequisites.

Finite RFC/vector/runtime checks corroborate exact bytes/counts/frames but prove neither universal equivalence nor standards compliance. Fresh controls reject wrong initialization, missing preparation skip, wrong event selection, extra conversion and stale prepared state. Those controls test the selected T74 source path; they do not prove universal callback transport.

## Remaining limitations and completion protocol

The proof is conditional on the explicitly accepted TCB and T49 normal-return scope. It does not establish arbitrary user AsRef/Drop termination or purity, preservation of user-global effects or consumed owned objects, cross-build callback reply equality, Rust contextual equivalence for stack/backtrace/timing observations, resource-exhaustion behavior, unsafe-invalid references, maximal allocation availability, arbitrary Engine overrides, malformed external encoded strings, insufficient buffers, unrepresentable lengths, streaming, SIMD or alternate alphabets/settings. These exclusions do not permit a supported codec error or panic.

All three protected Audit items are accepted for the selected claim. Campaign completion additionally requires exact installation of this Reviewer-authored report, independent installed hash confirmation, and successful `check_stage audit` with registered production, including its fresh preserved verifier replay. This report does not assert that those later completion actions have already passed.

# Unsuccessful Base64 verification attempt — operator limitation report

No public codec body bridge, caller effect or Base64 round-trip guarantee was established. The campaign passed neither Ground nor audit. No codec defect or counterexample was established. This report records an unsuccessful verification attempt; it is not an audit-certified final proof report. Acceptance of this report does not advance Ground or establish the operator’s original goal.

The unresolved facts concern verifier dictionary dependencies, original-mode/effect correspondence and consumer elaboration. They do not establish that the Base64 library is incorrect. The Manager stopped compiler expansion; t13 is reporting only.

## Objective and source identity

The [original request](/fastdata/v-boxiuli/noah/exp/tsC/verification/.system-proof/INPUT.md) asks for lossless composition of the actual public APIs, preserving arbitrary bytes, exact reported prefixes and both destination suffixes, with adequate space justified by the real sizing helpers and no unjustified errors or panics. The selected engine is `GeneralPurpose` with `general_purpose::STANDARD`, using `--no-default-features --features std` (std implies alloc). Direct Verus commands use `feature="std"` and `feature="alloc"`; default features and SIMD are not selected.

Production revision is **47fa113a5c309fd7dcf5618d0f738cc9056d4402**. Current read-only [state evidence](/fastdata/v-boxiuli/noah/exp/tsC/verification/.system-proof/t13/current-state.json) records that exact production HEAD and empty `git status --porcelain=v1 --untracked-files=all` on November 3, 2026 UTC: production is clean at capture time. Verification HEAD is the same revision, with the drift described below. The phase file is absent, meaning Ground by the guidance’s default. This task changes only the report, visible copy and t13 reporting evidence.

The [retained independent Reviewer letters](/fastdata/v-boxiuli/noah/exp/tsC/verification/.system-proof/t13/reviewer-letters.md) record acceptance of t1–t12 as bounded negative, diagnostic or prerequisite results, never acceptance of Ground or the original goal. The linked handoffs below are the independently reviewed evidence, not fresh t13 verifier runs.

## Established supporting facts and their limits

The [t1 contract interpretation](/fastdata/v-boxiuli/noah/exp/tsC/verification/t1-handoff/CONTRACT.md) supplies concrete open byte and frame relations in [round_trip_spec.rs](/fastdata/v-boxiuli/noah/exp/tsC/verification/src/round_trip_spec.rs). The [five supporting proofs](/fastdata/v-boxiuli/noah/exp/tsC/verification/.system-proof/t1/check-open-spec.rs), with [retained output](/fastdata/v-boxiuli/noah/exp/tsC/verification/.system-proof/t1/open-spec.log), cover exactly:

- For supported `n: usize`, mathematical encoded size is within isize, decoded capacity of that size lies between n and isize, and encoded size is divisible by four.
- For any `m: usize`, mathematical decoded capacity is within usize.
- `write_frame` determines one post-sequence for a fixed before-sequence and prefix; `encode_post` and `decode_post` determine unique counts and post-sequences when their respective relations hold.

These are two arithmetic and three conditional observation-uniqueness results. They establish neither existence of executable outcomes nor encoder injectivity, decoder inversion, actual helper arithmetic, public behavior or a consumer effect.

Let E(n)=4 ceil(n/3), D(m)=3 ceil(m/4), U=usize::MAX and I=isize::MAX. The representation argument in the t1 contract distinguishes helper-result representability, n <= 3 floor(U/4), from the supported round-trip slice domain, **0 <= n <= 3 floor(I/4)**, with legal complete byte slices and sufficient destinations. On the selected x86_64 route, the latter maximum is 6,917,529,027,641,081,853; E(max)=9,223,372,036,854,775,804 and D(E(max))=6,917,529,027,641,081,853. The helper-only maximum is 13,835,058,055,282,163,709. D(m) fits usize for usize m, but not every such capacity is a legal single slice. This is a symbolic representation argument, not an allocation promise or verification of actual helper implementations. Existing adequate buffers are premises; practical RAM limits do not narrow the promised byte domain.

[t3’s reviewed handoff](/fastdata/v-boxiuli/noah/exp/tsC/verification/t3-handoff/HANDOFF.md) established a narrow constant-initialization remedy with normal lifetime checking. [t4’s handoff](/fastdata/v-boxiuli/noah/exp/tsC/verification/t4-handoff/HANDOFF.md) established exact-bound AsRef translation, PADDING_SYMBOL translation and successful annotated `--no-verify --compile` build. Actual encode and caller verification then stopped at the dictionary cycle `Engine -> default encode_slice -> nested inner<E: Engine> -> Engine`, before ordinary body obligations. Annotation stripping and compilation support correspondence/integration only; neither is body verification.

[t5](/fastdata/v-boxiuli/noah/exp/tsC/verification/t5-handoff/HANDOFF.md) and [t6](/fastdata/v-boxiuli/noah/exp/tsC/verification/t6-handoff/HANDOFF.md) did not establish a sound acceptance remedy. [t7](/fastdata/v-boxiuli/noah/exp/tsC/verification/t7-handoff/HANDOFF.md), [t8](/fastdata/v-boxiuli/noah/exp/tsC/verification/t8-handoff/INTERFACE.md) and [t9](/fastdata/v-boxiuli/noah/exp/tsC/verification/t9-handoff/INTERFACE.md) retain instantiated provenance and incomplete structured diagnostic summaries. No diagnostic graph, export/import check or identity join establishes implementation correctness or consumer elaboration. **All 4,316 coarse acceptance edges remain**; no acceptance adapter or dependency removal was authorized or accepted.

[t10’s bounded imported leaf](/fastdata/v-boxiuli/noah/exp/tsC/verification/t10-handoff/HANDOFF.md) concerns only the actual `encode_with_padding` call to `usize::checked_add`. Its [selected interface](/fastdata/v-boxiuli/noah/exp/tsC/verification/t10-handoff/selected-interface.json) joins the source-linked Rust declaration and impl ancestry (zero generic parameters/predicates, two usize arguments, Option<usize> result, no higher-ranked binders), the imported assumed Verus contract, generated autospec companion, architecture context and call/substitution/dictionary provenance. The assumed returns contract distinguishes overflow from sum and has NoUnwind. Pinned identities and negative import controls support the diagnostic join. Leaf success/false-contract probes consume that assumed specification; they do not prove the Rust implementation. Pre-elaboration `fndef_axioms=None` does not exclude later generated obligations. Universal, FnDef/member-axiom and Fun consumer elaboration, including dictionary-before-member supply, remains unresolved; [t10 INTERFACE](/fastdata/v-boxiuli/noah/exp/tsC/verification/t10-handoff/INTERFACE.md) states that boundary.

[t11’s capability decision](/fastdata/v-boxiuli/noah/exp/tsC/verification/t11-handoff/DECISION.md) identifies mode-aware original-body scope/effect correspondence as separate from dictionary export. Its ownership probes distinguish borrowed cleanup from destruction of by-value generic input, including early-return and unwind paths; ordinary Rust MIR observations are not Verus correspondence proofs.

[t12’s negative D1 decision](/fastdata/v-boxiuli/noah/exp/tsC/verification/t12-handoff/DECISION.md) reaches the mandatory stopping boundary. All **30 omitted-body expressions lack modes**. The mixed body has 49 expressions: 4 Exec, 10 Spec, 7 Proof and **28 unknown**. Rust temporary scopes are observed, not a complete synthetic-local/drop/cleanup elaboration. Adjustment modes remain unknown rather than inherited. The omitted false assertion remains skipped. Fourteen differential cases and retained 516 passing regressions support unchanged observed outcomes; actual target/caller and logical cycles still reject. Provider traces establish relevant THIR/MIR ordering and initialization markers, not full query/cache or semantic correspondence. The existing producer cannot provide the missing modes under D1’s read-only boundary without broader work on code omitted from VIR. This does not prove that every alternative design is impossible.

## Requested APIs and behaviors still unproved

| Requested API or behavior | Disposition for the actual production code |
| --- | --- |
| `Engine::encode_slice`, including its selected GeneralPurpose STANDARD use and nested inner | No checked public body bridge; exact encoding/count, success, frame and panic obligations open. |
| `Engine::decode_slice`, including selected GeneralPurpose STANDARD use and nested inner | No checked public body bridge; recovery of every encoder output, exact count, frames and acceptance without error/panic open. |
| `encoded_len(n, true)` | Actual helper unproved, including overflow/None boundary, exact padded size and sufficiency on supported inputs. Temporary contract is not its implementation proof. |
| `decoded_len_estimate(m)` | Actual helper and GeneralPurpose estimate/forwarding unproved, including exact mathematical capacity and sufficiency for every produced encoded prefix. |
| Arbitrary byte sequences and representation-justified lengths | No implementation theorem across the symbolic domain, including empty, zero, non-UTF-8 and processing-block boundary lengths. Arithmetic support above does not close this obligation. |
| Generic input, conversion and Drop | Relational single-observation AsRef semantics, conversion/destruction effects, totality, panic and frame behavior remain P-GENERIC. No pure stable View or universal no-panic guarantee for arbitrary T was established. |
| Exact prefixes/counts and unchanged inputs | Encoder writes exactly encoding(B) and reports E(n); decoder consumes exactly that reported prefix, reports n and recovers bytes in order without loss/duplication; preservation of both borrowed inputs is unproved. |
| Both complete destination frames | Encode suffix and decode suffix preservation, independence from initial destination bytes and retention of encoded storage through decoding remain unproved. |
| Helpers and representation | Concrete STANDARD/table/config witness, padding, encode/decode internals and every material helper’s sufficiency/contract implementation remain unproved. |
| Successful public composition | The source-grounded caller is not verified: capacities, returned-prefix slicing, byte equality, lifecycle frames and absence of unjustified errors, overflow/index/debug-assertion or other codec panics remain open. |

B denotes the bytes returned by the wrapper’s single actual AsRef invocation, not a second conversion or a pre-state pure view. Sizing from an earlier conversion needs stability or a justified bound on the future observation. Arbitrary user conversion or Drop can mutate unrelated state, panic or diverge. Conditional codec reasoning after a valid conversion and total public-call guarantees therefore require distinct, justified user-code/environment interfaces. This observation is not permission to narrow arbitrary byte inputs or assume generic purity. The byte-slice representative [caller](/fastdata/v-boxiuli/noah/exp/tsC/verification/.system-proof/t1/caller.rs) does not replace the generic public obligation.

Remaining named obligations are P-GENERIC, P-GP-VIEW (including a lawful STANDARD witness), P-SIZES, P-PADDING, P-ENCODE, P-DECODE, both public bridges and P-CALLER. No internal error may be reclassified as permitted failure on supported round trips. External malformed strings, insufficient buffers, other configurations, interoperability, streaming and timing remain outside the original request.

## Production/verification drift and trust disclosure

The current [source manifest](/fastdata/v-boxiuli/noah/exp/tsC/verification/.system-proof/t13/source-manifest.json), [tracked drift patch](/fastdata/v-boxiuli/noah/exp/tsC/verification/.system-proof/t13/production-verification.patch) and [boundary inventory](/fastdata/v-boxiuli/noah/exp/tsC/verification/.system-proof/t13/boundaries.txt) expose current source state. Seven tracked files differ: alphabet.rs, encode.rs, decode.rs, engine/mod.rs, engine/general_purpose/mod.rs, engine/general_purpose/decode.rs and lib.rs. Additions include verifier datatype/trait/constant annotations, temporary contracts, vstd imports, `feature(sized_hierarchy)`, spec/proof module bindings and the diagnostic caller binding. New round_trip_spec.rs and round_trip_proof.rs are included in the manifest separately from the tracked patch. Task-local tools/evidence and visible handoffs also remain in verification. No production repair was made. Earlier enumerated stripping comparisons support unchanged original executable statements across 22 Rust files; annotated-build evidence is separately bounded and does not discharge semantic correspondence or proof obligations.

Temporary `external_body` boundaries remain at:

- `encoded_len` and `encode_with_padding`: assumed supported padded-size and encoding postconditions, respectively; neither body proved.
- `decoded_len_estimate`, Alphabet::from_str_unchecked, GeneralPurpose::new, internal_encode, internal_decoded_len_estimate, internal_decode, config, padding, GeneralPurposeConfig::new and encode_padding: unfinished sizing, table/config/constructor and forwarding bodies, including interfaces with no adequate semantic result contract.
- Engine defaults encode, encode_string, decode, decode_vec and decode_slice_unchecked: marked outside the selected public slice graph, not established by this campaign.

The [actual proof interface](/fastdata/v-boxiuli/noah/exp/tsC/verification/src/round_trip_proof.rs) retains opaque `is_standard` and `padding_enabled`. Engine::config and Config::encode_padding use temporary relations to those predicates; no concrete public witness makes them established STANDARD facts. They are implementation obligations, not accepted codec TCB. The AsRef external-trait specification exactly matches PointeeSized bounds and supplies translation only: no stable byte relation, purity, totality, Drop or protected-frame guarantee. The public slice targets have not been replaced by trusted wrappers. No absence of an external_body marker implies a successful proof.

The exact [t3 compiler patch](/fastdata/v-boxiuli/noah/exp/tsC/verification/t3-handoff/compiler.patch), SHA-256 **df755661374a7fb3753fdbaeff9ef27133c9592a288ceea1d7e6fd5ddc9d2d4c**, changes only `source/rustc_hir_analysis/src/lib.rs` and `source/rust_verify/src/verifier.rs`. It extracts the existing eager const/static initializer traversal into `check_crate_initializers` and calls it after mode analysis and `setup_verus_ctxt_for_thir_erasure`; normal type checking stays early and existing initializer checks remain. Upstream is d85f75780de687461a30e7771985c4a3e9ea0e58, Verus 0.2026.09.29.d85f757; patched version is dirty, using Rust 1.98.1 (48a229ceaefd4985c50990b14116b6d856af0985). [Tool identity](/fastdata/v-boxiuli/noah/exp/tsC/verification/t3-handoff/tool-identity.json) records binaries and support hashes. This is a compiler scheduling/trust delta, not a dictionary acceptance remedy. Original crash reproduction, patched minimal/valid-borrow success, E0515 dangling-reference rejection and E0080 invalid-unused-constant rejection supply limited regression support. They are not a compiler soundness certificate or universal early-query guarantee. Copied builtin/macros/vstd artifacts were unchanged; vstd correctness was not newly proved.

Later compiler lanes add diagnostic-only provenance, summary/interface export, original scope/mode observation and passive traces. They preserve t3 and conservative acceptance. [t10 patches/identity](/fastdata/v-boxiuli/noah/exp/tsC/verification/t10-handoff/tool-identity.json) and [t12 incremental patch](/fastdata/v-boxiuli/noah/exp/tsC/verification/t12-handoff/incremental.patch), with their reviewed handoffs, distinguish this instrumentation from acceptance changes. t10 and t12 are separate lanes, not a newly combined accepted compiler. Regression/differential evidence bounds observations, not soundness of a hypothetical elaboration remedy. The shared installed compiler route was not replaced. t12 discloses initial construction builds writing to the shared Cargo cache before its final isolated build; that cache activity is not a source or acceptance modification.

Environmental/trust assumptions for any future claim include valid Rust byte slices, aliasing/lifetime and ownership semantics, existing adequate storage, the pinned architecture/configuration, justified core/std imported specifications, vstd/builtin/macros, rustc/Verus (including declared local changes) and Z3. User AsRef/Drop behavior requires its own justified interface. No temporary codec contract is promoted to accepted TCB merely because compiler support is difficult. No complete accepted trust closure or fresh audit replay exists.

## Stopping reason and independent review

Further progress on the tested route requires broader compiler capability for instantiated dictionary dependencies, complete original-mode/effect correspondence and justified elaboration into actual consumers. The bounded imported checked_add contribution and D1 scope observations do not supply that capability. The Manager stopped compiler expansion beyond the authorized scope; this is the stopping reason, not a proof that every alternative route is impossible. No compiler implementation, proof repair, acceptance change or phase transition is part of t13.

Prior [attack decisions](/fastdata/v-boxiuli/noah/exp/tsC/verification/t1-handoff/ATTACK.md) remain bounded: analyze-function was operationally blocked by the Copilot route, analyze-protocol was not selected for the stateless uncertainty, and native specdet nested-target discovery supplied no determinism verdict or witness. Mathematical uniqueness does not replace these missing implementation connections. Reporting does not introduce a new codec hypothesis or counterexample.

The independent Reviewer should assess fidelity to the original objective, every API and requested behavior’s disposition, all drift/trust boundaries and the absence of success overclaims. The [t13 checks](/fastdata/v-boxiuli/noah/exp/tsC/verification/.system-proof/t13/checks.json) record link resolution, evidence hashes and byte identity of this report and its operator-visible copy. Accepting the report accepts an account of limitations only; Ground and the original goal remain unachieved.
任务原文（操作员请求）base64-v4-C/effective-objective.txt · 47 行 · 5,029 字节

Short goal:
Preserve arbitrary input bytes through the production Base64 encode_slice and decode_slice round trip, including exact reported prefixes, destination suffixes, generic input semantics, and the actual public callers.

Complete developer input (authoritative):
# Task: keep data unchanged through Base64 encoding and decoding

Use Verus to prove the properties below.

## What I need

I use this library to store or transmit binary data as text and recover the original data later. I want to verify that the real encoding and decoding APIs work together without losing, changing, duplicating or reordering bytes. This task is about lossless round-trip behavior, not a complete standards-compliance audit.

Use the registered production checkout at the fixed revision supplied with this task. The system-proof runtime provides that authoritative production checkout and a separate verification checkout of the same source. Treat every source path below as relative to the registered repository root; do not assume that either checkout is named `code/`. Read production to establish original behavior, make proof and verification adaptations in verification, and keep any authorized production fix and all production/verification drift explicit. Use the `GeneralPurpose` engine with `general_purpose::STANDARD`, disable default features, and enable only `std`: `--no-default-features --features std`. Do not use SIMD engines for this task.

## Top-level scope

| Entry point | What I use it for |
| --- | --- |
| `Engine::encode_slice` | Encode my input into a caller-provided buffer and report how many bytes were written |
| `Engine::decode_slice` | Decode exactly that reported encoded prefix into another caller-provided buffer |
| `encoded_len` and `decoded_len_estimate` | Size those buffers before making the two calls |

The public API is in `src/engine/mod.rs`. The selected implementation is in `src/engine/general_purpose/`; length/padding support and tables are in `src/encode.rs`, `src/decode.rs` and `src/alphabet.rs`. These supporting functions matter insofar as the real public calls depend on them; choose the internal breakdown yourself.

## Behavior I expect

1. For every input byte sequence within the supported length range, with buffers sized sufficiently using the public sizing helpers, encoding and subsequent decoding both succeed. The decoded length and every decoded byte equal the original input.
2. The caller passes only the encoded prefix reported by `encode_slice`, not unused capacity after it. Existing bytes in the output buffers must not influence the result.
3. Empty data, zero bytes, non-UTF-8 data and lengths on either side of the implementation's processing-block boundaries are all normal inputs. Do not limit the task to text strings or a few example lengths.
4. Input data is not modified. On success, bytes beyond the reported written prefix in each destination buffer remain unchanged.
5. State clearly which input lengths the platform and sizing helpers can represent. Within that range and the stated capacity conditions, reporting an error instead of recovering the data is not an acceptable substitute for success.

## When failure is permitted

No encoding or decoding error is permitted within the round-trip use described above: supported input length, sufficient caller-provided buffers, and decoding exactly the encoder's reported output. An error from an internal helper is not an additional permitted failure reason, and a panic is not a substitute for the required result.

Justify the supported length range from representation and capacity limits, not by defining it as whichever inputs the implementation accepts. Establish that the sizing helpers provide enough space in that range; do not assume their correctness to excuse a short output or a rejected encoding. Arbitrary external encoded strings, insufficient buffers and unrepresentable lengths remain outside this task, not exceptions that may be applied to supported calls.

## What is not part of this request

I am not asking for interoperability with another Base64 implementation, all RFC rules, arbitrary malformed-string behavior, exact error messages/offsets, insufficient-output behavior, streaming readers/writers, other alphabets/configurations, allocation internals or timing guarantees.

The decoder must work on every encoded string that this selected encoder produces for the inputs above. This does not require a promise about every other string the decoder could receive.

## What to return

Tell me which requested behaviors you established for these actual APIs, including the absence of unjustified errors in the supported round-trip, any counterexample or bug, the code and configuration covered, and remaining limitations or environmental assumptions. Keep the original revision identifiable. If code changes are needed, separate findings about the original from the changed version; do not replace the codec with another algorithm and report that as verification of this source.

规约与证明

round_trip_spec.rs：独立的往返规约base64-v4-C/proof/round_trip_spec.rs · 98 行 · 4,638 字节

在 GitHub 打开原文件

//! Open, caller-visible meaning for the STANDARD slice round trip.
//! No executable codec and no trusted operation bodies live here.
#![allow(missing_docs)]
use vstd::prelude::*;
verus! {
pub open spec fn encoded_size(n: int) -> int { 4 * ((n + 2) / 3) }
// Size for either lawful Engine padding choice; no global STANDARD premise.
pub open spec fn encoding_size(n: int, padding: bool) -> int {
    if padding { encoded_size(n) } else { 4 * (n / 3) + if n % 3 == 0 { 0 } else { n % 3 + 1 } }
}
// Timeless admitted-reply relation, NOT an invocation identity or pure View.
// Public codec calls separately export their actual snapshot in an exclusive
// erased event slot; only that captured snapshot indexes their guarantees.
pub open spec fn observed<T: AsRef<[u8]>>(input: T, bytes: &[u8]) -> bool {
    call_ensures(<T as AsRef<[u8]>>::as_ref, (&input,), bytes)
}
// Optional sufficient admission envelope for legacy callers; not a public
// codec precondition and not required for actual-event lawfulness.
pub open spec fn encode_domain<T: AsRef<[u8]>>(input: T) -> bool {
    forall|bytes: &[u8]| #[trigger] observed(input, bytes) ==> supported(bytes@.len() as int)
}
pub open spec fn decoded_capacity(m: int) -> int { 3 * ((m + 3) / 4) }
pub open spec fn supported(n: int) -> bool {
    0 <= n <= 3 * ((isize::MAX as int) / 4)
}
pub open spec fn encoded_helper_post(n: int, r: Option<usize>) -> bool {
    if encoded_size(n) <= usize::MAX as int {
        r == Some(encoded_size(n) as usize)
    } else { r == None }
}
pub open spec fn decoded_helper_post(m: int, r: usize) -> bool {
    r as int == decoded_capacity(m)
}
pub open spec fn symbol(v: int) -> u8 {
    if v < 26 { (65 + v) as u8 }
    else if v < 52 { (97 + v - 26) as u8 }
    else if v < 62 { (48 + v - 52) as u8 }
    else if v == 62 { 43u8 } else { 47u8 }
}
pub open spec fn byte_or_zero(b: Seq<u8>, k: int) -> int {
    if 0 <= k < b.len() { b[k] as int } else { 0 }
}
pub open spec fn encoded_byte(b: Seq<u8>, i: int) -> u8 {
    let j = 3 * (i / 4);
    let a = byte_or_zero(b, j);
    let c = byte_or_zero(b, j + 1);
    let d = byte_or_zero(b, j + 2);
    if i % 4 == 0 { symbol(a / 4) }
    else if i % 4 == 1 { symbol((a % 4) * 16 + c / 16) }
    else if i % 4 == 2 {
        if j + 1 >= b.len() { 61u8 } else { symbol((c % 16) * 4 + d / 64) }
    } else {
        if j + 2 >= b.len() { 61u8 } else { symbol(d % 64) }
    }
}
pub open spec fn encoding(b: Seq<u8>) -> Seq<u8> {
    Seq::new(encoded_size(b.len() as int) as nat, |i: int| encoded_byte(b, i))
}
pub open spec fn write_frame(before: Seq<u8>, after: Seq<u8>, prefix: Seq<u8>) -> bool {
    &&& before.len() == after.len()
    &&& prefix.len() <= after.len()
    &&& after.take(prefix.len() as int) == prefix
    &&& forall|i: int| prefix.len() <= i < after.len() ==> after[i] == before[i]
}
pub open spec fn encode_post(b: Seq<u8>, before: Seq<u8>, after: Seq<u8>, written: int) -> bool {
    written == encoded_size(b.len() as int) && write_frame(before, after, encoding(b))
}
pub open spec fn canonical_input(input: Seq<u8>) -> bool {
    exists|b: Seq<u8>| supported(b.len() as int) && encoding(b) == input
}
/// Optional sufficient admission envelope for legacy callers. Actual-event
/// canonicality/capacity is sufficient even when unrelated admitted replies fail.
pub open spec fn decode_domain<T: AsRef<[u8]>>(input: T, capacity: int) -> bool {
    forall|bytes: &[u8]| #[trigger] observed(input, bytes)
        ==> canonical_input(bytes@) && capacity >= decoded_capacity(bytes.len() as int)
}
/// Lawfulness of the one snapshot actually consumed by an invocation.
/// These predicates say nothing about another conversion, admission, or context.
pub open spec fn encode_event_lawful(bytes: Seq<u8>, capacity: int) -> bool {
    supported(bytes.len() as int) && capacity >= encoded_size(bytes.len() as int)
}
pub open spec fn decode_event_lawful(bytes: Seq<u8>, capacity: int) -> bool {
    canonical_input(bytes) && capacity >= decoded_capacity(bytes.len() as int)
}
pub open spec fn decode_post(b: Seq<u8>, before: Seq<u8>, after: Seq<u8>, written: int) -> bool {
    written == b.len() && write_frame(before, after, b)
}
pub open spec fn round_trip_post(
    b: Seq<u8>, b_after: Seq<u8>, encoded_before: Seq<u8>, encoded_after: Seq<u8>,
    decoded_before: Seq<u8>, decoded_after: Seq<u8>, encode_ok: bool, decode_ok: bool,
    encoded_written: int, decoded_written: int,
) -> bool {
    &&& b_after == b
    &&& encode_ok && decode_ok
    &&& encode_post(b, encoded_before, encoded_after, encoded_written)
    &&& decode_post(b, decoded_before, decoded_after, decoded_written)
}
}

//! Derived from Engine::encode_slice's prefix-truncating doctest and
//! engine/tests.rs's encode/decode slice composition and suffix observations.
use crate::{engine::general_purpose::STANDARD, Engine, encoded_len, decoded_len_estimate};
use crate::round_trip_spec::*;
use vstd::prelude::*;
verus! {
#[verus_spec(
    requires
        supported(input@.len() as int),
        encoded@.len() >= encoded_size(input@.len() as int),
        decoded@.len() >= decoded_capacity(encoded_size(input@.len() as int)),
    ensures
        final(encoded)@.take(encoded_size(input@.len() as int)) == encoding(input@),
        final(decoded)@.take(input@.len() as int) == input@,
        write_frame(old(encoded)@, final(encoded)@, encoding(input@)),
        write_frame(old(decoded)@, final(decoded)@, input@),
)]
pub fn caller(input: &[u8], encoded: &mut [u8], decoded: &mut [u8]) {
    proof! {
        crate::round_trip_proof::sizes(input.len());
        assert(crate::round_trip_spec::encode_domain(input));
    }
    let capacity = encoded_len(input.len(), true).unwrap();
    assert!(capacity <= encoded.len());
    let ghost input_before = input@;
    let ghost encoded_before = encoded@;
    let ghost decoded_before = decoded@;
    let written = { let tracked mut call_event = Ghost(Seq::<u8>::empty()); #[verus_spec(with Tracked(&mut call_event))] STANDARD.encode_slice(input, encoded) }.unwrap();
    proof! {
        let bytes = choose|bytes: &[u8]| crate::round_trip_spec::observed(input, bytes)
            && crate::round_trip_spec::encode_post(input@, encoded_before, encoded@, written as int);
        assert(crate::round_trip_spec::encode_post(input@, encoded_before, encoded@, written as int));
    }
    assert!(written == capacity);
    // Only the reported prefix is supplied to the decoder.
    let prefix = &encoded[..written];
    let decode_capacity = decoded_len_estimate(prefix.len());
    assert!(decode_capacity <= decoded.len());
    proof! {
        assert(prefix@ == crate::round_trip_spec::encoding(input@));
        assert(crate::round_trip_spec::canonical_input(prefix@));
        assert(crate::decoder_contract::image(input@, prefix@));
    }
    let recovered = { let tracked mut call_event = Ghost(Seq::<u8>::empty()); #[verus_spec(with Tracked(&mut call_event))] STANDARD.decode_slice(prefix, decoded) }.unwrap();
    proof! {
        let observed = choose|observed: &[u8]| crate::round_trip_spec::observed(prefix, observed)
            && (forall|b: Seq<u8>| #[trigger] crate::decoder_contract::image(b, observed@)
                ==> crate::round_trip_spec::decode_post(b, decoded_before, decoded@, recovered as int));
        assert(observed@ == prefix@);
        assert(crate::decoder_contract::image(input@, observed@));
        assert(crate::round_trip_spec::decode_post(input@, decoded_before, decoded@, recovered as int));
    }
    assert!(recovered == input.len());
    assert!(&decoded[..recovered] == input);
    proof! {
        assert(input@ == input_before);
        assert(round_trip_post(input_before, input@, encoded_before, encoded@,
            decoded_before, decoded@, true, true, written as int, recovered as int));
    }
}

}

//! Internal proof interfaces. Every opaque item here is a named unfinished obligation.
use vstd::prelude::*;
verus! {
// P-SIZES arithmetic from t1/check-open-spec.rs, now consumed by the actual helper.
pub proof fn sizes(n: usize)
    requires crate::round_trip_spec::supported(n as int),
    ensures
        0 <= crate::round_trip_spec::encoded_size(n as int) <= isize::MAX as int,
        n as int <= crate::round_trip_spec::decoded_capacity(
            crate::round_trip_spec::encoded_size(n as int)) <= isize::MAX as int,
        crate::round_trip_spec::encoded_size(n as int) % 4 == 0,
{}

// Exact unpadded Base64 count; the GeneralPurpose producer is checked in t18,
// conditional on the explicit unfinished read_u64 leaf and adaptation correspondence.
pub open spec fn unpadded_size(n: int) -> int {
    4 * (n / 3) + if n % 3 == 0 { 0 } else { n % 3 + 1 }
}
pub open spec fn padding_count(k: int) -> int { (4 - k % 4) % 4 }

pub proof fn padding_sizes(n: usize, k: usize)
    requires crate::round_trip_spec::supported(n as int),
        k as int == unpadded_size(n as int),
    ensures
        0 <= k <= crate::round_trip_spec::encoded_size(n as int),
        0 <= padding_count(k as int) <= 2,
        k + padding_count(k as int) == crate::round_trip_spec::encoded_size(n as int),
        k + padding_count(k as int) <= usize::MAX,
{
    sizes(n);
}

// The canonical trailing positions are '='; no premise about internal encoder bytes.
pub proof fn canonical_padding(b: Seq<u8>, k: usize)
    requires crate::round_trip_spec::supported(b.len() as int),
        k as int == unpadded_size(b.len() as int),
    ensures forall|j: int| k <= j < crate::round_trip_spec::encoded_size(b.len() as int)
        ==> crate::round_trip_spec::encoded_byte(b, j) == 61u8,
{
    assert forall|j: int| k <= j < crate::round_trip_spec::encoded_size(b.len() as int)
        implies crate::round_trip_spec::encoded_byte(b, j) == 61u8 by {
    }
}

// Always callable at the public path; exports only a conditional consequence.
// This does not claim that the executed internal encoder establishes the antecedent.
pub proof fn padding_sizes_if(n: usize, k: usize)
    ensures (n <= isize::MAX
        && k as int == unpadded_size(n as int)) ==>
        (0 <= k <= crate::round_trip_spec::encoded_size(n as int)
        && 0 <= padding_count(k as int) <= 2
        && k + padding_count(k as int) == crate::round_trip_spec::encoded_size(n as int)
        && k + padding_count(k as int) <= usize::MAX),
{
    if n <= isize::MAX && k as int == unpadded_size(n as int) {
    }
}

// T52-RUST-BYTE-SLICE-VALIDITY (new explicit library/unsafe-validity TCB).
// Rust Reference wide-reference validity forbids pointed-to size > isize::MAX;
// slice::from_raw_parts likewise requires this bound. For u8, size_of is one. This
// interface applies only to actual valid &[u8], including valid unsafe-created
// slices, never arbitrary Seq<u8>, usize lengths, or hypothetical allocations.
// vstd currently exports only the weaker usize bound. Independent review owed.
#[verifier::external_body]
pub proof fn byte_slice_size_bound(bytes: &[u8])
    ensures bytes@.len() <= isize::MAX as int,
{}

pub proof fn byte_slice_encoding_fits(n: usize, padding: bool)
    requires n <= isize::MAX,
    ensures crate::round_trip_spec::encoding_size(n as int, padding) <= usize::MAX as int,
{}

// A physical padded destination can hold only supported source lengths.
pub proof fn padded_capacity_supported(n: usize, capacity: usize)
    requires capacity <= isize::MAX,
        capacity as int >= crate::round_trip_spec::encoded_size(n as int),
    ensures crate::round_trip_spec::supported(n as int),
{}

// Encoding table meaning is now a type-owned predicate; STANDARD obtains it
// from the accepted concrete T21 table witness (not an uninterpreted capability).
pub open spec fn is_standard<E: crate::engine::Engine + ?Sized>(engine: &E) -> bool {
    engine.encoding_padding() && engine.canonical_encoding_table() && engine.padding_symbol().byte() == 61u8
}
// P-GENERIC-INTERFACE: translation only. No relation to a stable pure View,
// no totality/no-panic claim, no interior-effect or Drop/frame guarantee.
#[verifier::external_trait_specification]
pub trait ExAsRef<T: core::marker::PointeeSized>: core::marker::PointeeSized {
    type ExternalTraitSpecificationFor: core::convert::AsRef<T>;
    fn as_ref(&self) -> &T;
}
// P-STD-ASREF: exact core implementations, assumed library interfaces only.
// The slice implementation returns self; the reference implementation delegates once.
#[verifier::external_fn_specification]
pub fn slice_as_ref<T>(s: &[T]) -> (r: &[T])
    ensures r@ == s@
{ <[T] as AsRef<[T]>>::as_ref(s) }
#[verifier::external_fn_specification]
pub fn reference_as_ref<'a, 'b,
    T: core::marker::PointeeSized + AsRef<U>, U: core::marker::PointeeSized>
    (s: &'b &'a T) -> (r: &'b U)
    ensures call_ensures(<T as AsRef<U>>::as_ref, (*s,), r)
{ <&'a T as AsRef<U>>::as_ref(s) }

// P-STD-ASSERT-FAILED: core panic path; require it unreachable rather than
// granting any successful effect. The runtime function and formatting remain intact.
#[verifier::external_type_specification]
pub struct ExAssertKind(core::panicking::AssertKind);
#[verifier::external_fn_specification]
pub fn ex_assert_failed<T: core::fmt::Debug + ?Sized, U: core::fmt::Debug + ?Sized>(
        kind: core::panicking::AssertKind, left: &T, right: &U,
        args: Option<core::fmt::Arguments<'_>>,
    ) -> !
    requires false
{ core::panicking::assert_failed(kind, left, right, args) }
pub proof fn masked_u64(x: u64)
    ensures
        (x >> 58u64) & 63u64 < 64,
        (x >> 52u64) & 63u64 < 64,
        (x >> 46u64) & 63u64 < 64,
        (x >> 40u64) & 63u64 < 64,
        (x >> 34u64) & 63u64 < 64,
        (x >> 28u64) & 63u64 < 64,
        (x >> 22u64) & 63u64 < 64,
        (x >> 16u64) & 63u64 < 64,
{ assert((x >> 58u64) & 63u64 < 64 && (x >> 52u64) & 63u64 < 64 && (x >> 46u64) & 63u64 < 64 && (x >> 40u64) & 63u64 < 64 && (x >> 34u64) & 63u64 < 64 && (x >> 28u64) & 63u64 < 64 && (x >> 22u64) & 63u64 < 64 && (x >> 16u64) & 63u64 < 64) by(bit_vector); }
pub proof fn byte_indices(a: u8, b: u8, c: u8)
    ensures
        a >> 2u8 < 64,
        ((a << 4u8 | b >> 4u8) & 63u8) < 64,
        ((b << 2u8 | c >> 6u8) & 63u8) < 64,
        c & 63u8 < 64,
        (a << 4u8) & 63u8 < 64,
        (b << 2u8) & 63u8 < 64,
{ assert(a >> 2u8 < 64 && ((a << 4u8 | b >> 4u8) & 63u8) < 64 && ((b << 2u8 | c >> 6u8) & 63u8) < 64 && c & 63u8 < 64 && (a << 4u8) & 63u8 < 64 && (b << 2u8) & 63u8 < 64) by(bit_vector); }
}

//! Shared conditional decoder composition interfaces. Lower bodies remain obligations.
#![allow(missing_docs)]
use vstd::prelude::*;
use crate::engine::{DecodeMetadata, DecodePaddingMode};
use crate::DecodeSliceError;
verus! {
pub open spec fn table_ok(t: Seq<u8>) -> bool {
    t.len() == 256 && forall|v: int| 0 <= v < 64 ==>
        #[trigger] t[crate::round_trip_spec::symbol(v) as int] == v
}
pub open spec fn config_ok(p: crate::alphabet::Symbol, m: DecodePaddingMode, allow: bool) -> bool {
    p.byte() == 61 && m == DecodePaddingMode::RequireCanonical && !allow
}
pub open spec fn image(b: Seq<u8>, input: Seq<u8>) -> bool {
    crate::round_trip_spec::supported(b.len() as int)
    && input == crate::round_trip_spec::encoding(b)
}
pub open spec fn boundary(m: int) -> int { if m == 0 { 0 } else { 4 * ((m - 1) / 4) } }
pub open spec fn metadata(b: Seq<u8>, r: Result<DecodeMetadata, DecodeSliceError>) -> bool {
    r.is_ok() && r.unwrap().count() == b.len()
    && r.unwrap().first_padding() == if b.len() % 3 == 0 { None }
       else { Some(crate::round_trip_proof::unpadded_size(b.len() as int) as usize) }
}
// Exact range write and complete destination frame; also used by nonzero SIMD prefixes.
pub open spec fn range_write(before: Seq<u8>, after: Seq<u8>, b: Seq<u8>, lo: int, hi: int) -> bool {
    &&& before.len() == after.len()
    &&& 0 <= lo <= hi <= b.len()
    &&& hi <= after.len()
    &&& forall|j: int| lo <= j < hi ==> after[j] == b[j]
    &&& forall|j: int| 0 <= j < after.len() && !(lo <= j < hi) ==> after[j] == before[j]
}
pub proof fn compose(before: Seq<u8>, middle: Seq<u8>, after: Seq<u8>, b: Seq<u8>, k: int)
    requires range_write(before, middle, b, 0, k), range_write(middle, after, b, k, b.len() as int),
    ensures crate::round_trip_spec::write_frame(before, after, b),
{
    assert(after.take(b.len() as int) =~= b);
}
pub proof fn image_bounds(b: Seq<u8>, input: Seq<u8>)
    requires image(b, input),
    ensures input.len() % 4 == 0,
        0 <= boundary(input.len() as int) <= input.len(),
        boundary(input.len() as int) % 4 == 0,
        0 <= boundary(input.len() as int) / 4 * 3 <= b.len(),
        input.len() - boundary(input.len() as int) <= 4,
        b.len() <= crate::round_trip_spec::decoded_capacity(input.len() as int),
{}
// Public Result::map projection of the same metadata/frame guarantee.
pub proof fn public_projection(input: Seq<u8>, before: Seq<u8>, after: Seq<u8>,
    r: Result<DecodeMetadata, DecodeSliceError>)
    requires exists|b: Seq<u8>| image(b, input),

+//! Shared conditional decoder composition interfaces. Lower bodies remain obligations.
+#![allow(missing_docs)]
+use vstd::prelude::*;
+use crate::engine::{DecodeMetadata, DecodePaddingMode};
+use crate::DecodeSliceError;
+verus! {
+pub open spec fn table_ok(t: Seq<u8>) -> bool {
+    t.len() == 256 && forall|v: int| 0 <= v < 64 ==>
+        #[trigger] t[crate::round_trip_spec::symbol(v) as int] == v
+}
+pub open spec fn config_ok(p: crate::alphabet::Symbol, m: DecodePaddingMode, allow: bool) -> bool {
+    p.byte() == 61 && m == DecodePaddingMode::RequireCanonical && !allow
+}
+pub open spec fn image(b: Seq<u8>, input: Seq<u8>) -> bool {
+    crate::round_trip_spec::supported(b.len() as int)
+    && input == crate::round_trip_spec::encoding(b)
+}
+pub open spec fn boundary(m: int) -> int { if m == 0 { 0 } else { 4 * ((m - 1) / 4) } }
+pub open spec fn metadata(b: Seq<u8>, r: Result<DecodeMetadata, DecodeSliceError>) -> bool {
+    r.is_ok() && r.unwrap().count() == b.len()
+    && r.unwrap().first_padding() == if b.len() % 3 == 0 { None }
+       else { Some(crate::round_trip_proof::unpadded_size(b.len() as int) as usize) }
+}
+// Exact range write and complete destination frame; also used by nonzero SIMD prefixes.
+pub open spec fn range_write(before: Seq<u8>, after: Seq<u8>, b: Seq<u8>, lo: int, hi: int) -> bool {
+    &&& before.len() == after.len()
+    &&& 0 <= lo <= hi <= b.len()
+    &&& hi <= after.len()
+    &&& forall|j: int| lo <= j < hi ==> after[j] == b[j]
+    &&& forall|j: int| 0 <= j < after.len() && !(lo <= j < hi) ==> after[j] == before[j]
+}
+pub proof fn compose(before: Seq<u8>, middle: Seq<u8>, after: Seq<u8>, b: Seq<u8>, k: int)
+    requires range_write(before, middle, b, 0, k), range_write(middle, after, b, k, b.len() as int),
+    ensures crate::round_trip_spec::write_frame(before, after, b),
+{
+    assert(after.take(b.len() as int) =~= b);
+}
+pub proof fn image_bounds(b: Seq<u8>, input: Seq<u8>)
+    requires image(b, input),
+    ensures input.len() % 4 == 0,
+        0 <= boundary(input.len() as int) <= input.len(),
+        boundary(input.len() as int) % 4 == 0,
+        0 <= boundary(input.len() as int) / 4 * 3 <= b.len(),
+        input.len() - boundary(input.len() as int) <= 4,
+        b.len() <= crate::round_trip_spec::decoded_capacity(input.len() as int),
+{}
+// Public Result::map projection of the same metadata/frame guarantee.
+pub proof fn public_projection(input: Seq<u8>, before: Seq<u8>, after: Seq<u8>,
+    r: Result<DecodeMetadata, DecodeSliceError>)
+    requires exists|b: Seq<u8>| image(b, input),
+        forall|b: Seq<u8>| #[trigger] image(b, input) ==> metadata(b, r)

+//! Public decode usage on arbitrary canonical encoder images.
+//! Follows Engine slice API documentation and tests; does not execute an encoder.
+#![allow(missing_docs)]
+use vstd::prelude::*;

+verus! {
+/// Canonical image and sufficient physical destination are the only caller premises.
+pub fn standard_decode_image(
+    input: &[u8], output: &mut [u8], Ghost(original): Ghost<Seq<u8>>,
+) -> (written: usize)
+    requires
+        crate::decoder_contract::image(original, input@),
+        old(output).len() >= crate::round_trip_spec::decoded_capacity(input.len() as int),
+    ensures
+        written == original.len(),
+        crate::round_trip_spec::write_frame(old(output)@, final(output)@, original),
+{
+    let ghost before = output@;
+    let capacity = decoded_len_estimate(input.len());
+    assert!(capacity <= output.len());
+    proof {
+        assert(crate::round_trip_spec::canonical_input(input@));
+        assert(crate::round_trip_spec::decode_domain(input, output.len() as int));
+    }
+    let engine = STANDARD;
+    proof { engine.decoder_readiness(); }
+    let written = { let tracked mut call_event = Ghost(Seq::<u8>::empty()); #[verus_spec(with Tracked(&mut call_event))] engine.decode_slice(input, output) }.unwrap();
+    proof {
+        let observed = choose|observed: &[u8]|
+            crate::round_trip_spec::observed(input, observed)
+            && (forall|b: Seq<u8>| #[trigger] crate::decoder_contract::image(b, observed@)
+                ==> crate::round_trip_spec::decode_post(b, before, output@, written as int));
+        assert(observed@ == input@);
+        assert(crate::decoder_contract::image(original, observed@));
+        assert(crate::round_trip_spec::decode_post(original, before, output@, written as int));
+    }
+    written
+}

+//! Conditional fixed-chunk interface; the decode-table producer is separate.
+use vstd::prelude::*;
+verus! {
+pub open spec fn image(input: Seq<u8>, b: Seq<u8>) -> bool {
+    input.len() >= 4 * (b.len() / 3) &&
+    forall|i: int| 0 <= i < 4 * (b.len() / 3) ==>
+        input[i] == crate::round_trip_spec::encoded_byte(b, i)
+}
+pub open spec fn inverse(table: Seq<u8>) -> bool {
+    table.len() == 256 && forall|v: int| 0 <= v < 64 ==>
+        table[crate::round_trip_spec::symbol(v) as int] == v
+}
+pub open spec fn sextet(b: Seq<u8>, i: int) -> int {
+    let j = 3 * (i / 4);
+    if i % 4 == 0 { b[j] as int / 4 }
+    else if i % 4 == 1 { (b[j] as int % 4) * 16 + b[j+1] as int / 16 }
+    else if i % 4 == 2 { (b[j+1] as int % 16) * 4 + b[j+2] as int / 64 }
+    else { b[j+2] as int % 64 }
+}
+pub proof fn image_sextets(input: Seq<u8>, table: Seq<u8>, b: Seq<u8>)
+    requires (b.len() == 3 || b.len() == 6), inverse(table), image(input,b),
+    ensures forall|i: int| 0 <= i < 4 * (b.len() / 3) ==>
+        table[input[i] as int] == sextet(b,i) && table[input[i] as int] < 64,
+{
+    assert forall|i: int| 0 <= i < 4 * (b.len() / 3) implies
+        table[input[i] as int] == sextet(b,i) && table[input[i] as int] < 64 by {
+        let v = sextet(b,i);
+        assert(0 <= 3 * (i / 4) && 3 * (i / 4) + 2 < b.len());
+        assert(0 <= v < 64);
+        assert(input[i] == crate::round_trip_spec::symbol(v));
+        assert(table[crate::round_trip_spec::symbol(v) as int] == v);
+    }
+}
+// STD-ENDIAN32/64: explicit library TCB only. These wrappers execute exactly
+// the original to_be_bytes operation; no codec steps are trusted. The direct
+// external_fn_specification route fails on core's anonymous return-length const.
+#[inline(always)]
+#[verifier::external_body]
+pub fn be32(x: u32) -> (r: [u8; 4])
+    ensures forall|i: int| 0 <= i < 4 ==> r[i] == ((x >> ((3-i)*8) as u32) & 255) as u8,
+{ x.to_be_bytes() }
+#[inline(always)]
+#[verifier::external_body]
+pub fn be64(x: u64) -> (r: [u8; 8])
+    ensures forall|i: int| 0 <= i < 8 ==> r[i] == ((x >> ((7-i)*8) as u64) & 255) as u8,
+{ x.to_be_bytes() }
+pub proof fn unpack32(b0: u8, b1: u8, b2: u8)
+    ensures { let x: u32 = ((((b0 as u32) / 4) as u32) << 26) | ((((b0 as u32) % 4 * 16 + (b1 as u32) / 16) as u32) << 20) | ((((b1 as u32) % 16 * 4 + (b2 as u32) / 64) as u32) << 14) | ((((b2 as u32) % 64) as u32) << 8); ((x >> 24) & 255) as u8 == b0 && ((x >> 16) & 255) as u8 == b1 && ((x >> 8) & 255) as u8 == b2 },
+{
+    assert({ let x: u32 = ((((b0 as u32) / 4) as u32) << 26) | ((((b0 as u32) % 4 * 16 + (b1 as u32) / 16) as u32) << 20) | ((((b1 as u32) % 16 * 4 + (b2 as u32) / 64) as u32) << 14) | ((((b2 as u32) % 64) as u32) << 8); ((x >> 24) & 255) as u8 == b0 && ((x >> 16) & 255) as u8 == b1 && ((x >> 8) & 255) as u8 == b2 }) by(bit_vector);
+}
+
+pub open spec fn packed32(b: Seq<u8>) -> u32 {
+    ((sextet(b,0) as u32) << 26) | ((sextet(b,1) as u32) << 20) | ((sextet(b,2) as u32) << 14) | ((sextet(b,3) as u32) << 8)
+}
+pub proof fn recover32(b: Seq<u8>, x: u32)
+    requires b.len() == 3, x == packed32(b),
+    ensures ((x >> 24) & 255) as u8 == b[0], ((x >> 16) & 255) as u8 == b[1], ((x >> 8) & 255) as u8 == b[2],
+{
+    unpack32(b[0], b[1], b[2]);
+}
+
+pub proof fn unpack64(b0: u8, b1: u8, b2: u8, b3: u8, b4: u8, b5: u8)
+    ensures { let x: u64 = ((((b0 as u64) / 4) as u64) << 58) | ((((b0 as u64) % 4 * 16 + (b1 as u64) / 16) as u64) << 52) | ((((b1 as u64) % 16 * 4 + (b2 as u64) / 64) as u64) << 46) | ((((b2 as u64) % 64) as u64) << 40) | ((((b3 as u64) / 4) as u64) << 34) | ((((b3 as u64) % 4 * 16 + (b4 as u64) / 16) as u64) << 28) | ((((b4 as u64) % 16 * 4 + (b5 as u64) / 64) as u64) << 22) | ((((b5 as u64) % 64) as u64) << 16); ((x >> 56) & 255) as u8 == b0 && ((x >> 48) & 255) as u8 == b1 && ((x >> 40) & 255) as u8 == b2 && ((x >> 32) & 255) as u8 == b3 && ((x >> 24) & 255) as u8 == b4 && ((x >> 16) & 255) as u8 == b5 },
+{
+    assert({ let x: u64 = ((((b0 as u64) / 4) as u64) << 58) | ((((b0 as u64) % 4 * 16 + (b1 as u64) / 16) as u64) << 52) | ((((b1 as u64) % 16 * 4 + (b2 as u64) / 64) as u64) << 46) | ((((b2 as u64) % 64) as u64) << 40) | ((((b3 as u64) / 4) as u64) << 34) | ((((b3 as u64) % 4 * 16 + (b4 as u64) / 16) as u64) << 28) | ((((b4 as u64) % 16 * 4 + (b5 as u64) / 64) as u64) << 22) | ((((b5 as u64) % 64) as u64) << 16); ((x >> 56) & 255) as u8 == b0 && ((x >> 48) & 255) as u8 == b1 && ((x >> 40) & 255) as u8 == b2 && ((x >> 32) & 255) as u8 == b3 && ((x >> 24) & 255) as u8 == b4 && ((x >> 16) & 255) as u8 == b5 }) by(bit_vector);
+}
+
+pub open spec fn packed64(b: Seq<u8>) -> u64 {
+    ((sextet(b,0) as u64) << 58) | ((sextet(b,1) as u64) << 52) | ((sextet(b,2) as u64) << 46) | ((sextet(b,3) as u64) << 40) | ((sextet(b,4) as u64) << 34) | ((sextet(b,5) as u64) << 28) | ((sextet(b,6) as u64) << 22) | ((sextet(b,7) as u64) << 16)
+}
+pub proof fn recover64(b: Seq<u8>, x: u64)
+    requires b.len() == 6, x == packed64(b),
+    ensures ((x >> 56) & 255) as u8 == b[0], ((x >> 48) & 255) as u8 == b[1], ((x >> 40) & 255) as u8 == b[2], ((x >> 32) & 255) as u8 == b[3], ((x >> 24) & 255) as u8 == b[4], ((x >> 16) & 255) as u8 == b[5],
+{
+    unpack64(b[0], b[1], b[2], b[3], b[4], b[5]);
+}
+
+pub proof fn sextet_unpack32(v0: u8, v1: u8, v2: u8, v3: u8)
+    requires v0 < 64, v1 < 64, v2 < 64, v3 < 64,
+    ensures { let x: u32 = ((v0 as u32) << 26) | ((v1 as u32) << 20) | ((v2 as u32) << 14) | ((v3 as u32) << 8); ((x >> 24) & 255) as u8 == ((v0 as u32) * 4 + (v1 as u32) / 16) as u8 && ((x >> 16) & 255) as u8 == ((v1 as u32) % 16 * 16 + (v2 as u32) / 4) as u8 && ((x >> 8) & 255) as u8 == ((v2 as u32) % 4 * 64 + (v3 as u32)) as u8 },
+{
+    assert({ let x: u32 = ((v0 as u32) << 26) | ((v1 as u32) << 20) | ((v2 as u32) << 14) | ((v3 as u32) << 8); ((x >> 24) & 255) as u8 == ((v0 as u32) * 4 + (v1 as u32) / 16) as u8 && ((x >> 16) & 255) as u8 == ((v1 as u32) % 16 * 16 + (v2 as u32) / 4) as u8 && ((x >> 8) & 255) as u8 == ((v2 as u32) % 4 * 64 + (v3 as u32)) as u8 }) by(bit_vector)
+        requires v0 < 64, v1 < 64, v2 < 64, v3 < 64;
+}
+pub open spec fn table_pack32(input: Seq<u8>,table: Seq<u8>) -> u32 {
+    ((table[input[0] as int] as u32) << 26) | ((table[input[1] as int] as u32) << 20) | ((table[input[2] as int] as u32) << 14) | ((table[input[3] as int] as u32) << 8)
+}
+pub proof fn table_unpack32(input: Seq<u8>,table: Seq<u8>,x: u32)
+    requires super::decode::quad_valid(input,table,0,4), x == table_pack32(input,table),
+    ensures ((x >> 24) & 255) as u8 == super::decode::quad_byte(input,table,0), ((x >> 16) & 255) as u8 == super::decode::quad_byte(input,table,1), ((x >> 8) & 255) as u8 == super::decode::quad_byte(input,table,2),
+{
+    reveal(super::decode::quad_byte);
+    sextet_unpack32(table[input[0] as int], table[input[1] as int], table[input[2] as int], table[input[3] as int]);
+}
+
+pub proof fn sextet_unpack64(v0: u8, v1: u8, v2: u8, v3: u8, v4: u8, v5: u8, v6: u8, v7: u8)
+    requires v0 < 64, v1 < 64, v2 < 64, v3 < 64, v4 < 64, v5 < 64, v6 < 64, v7 < 64,
+    ensures { let x: u64 = ((v0 as u64) << 58) | ((v1 as u64) << 52) | ((v2 as u64) << 46) | ((v3 as u64) << 40) | ((v4 as u64) << 34) | ((v5 as u64) << 28) | ((v6 as u64) << 22) | ((v7 as u64) << 16); ((x >> 56) & 255) as u8 == ((v0 as u64) * 4 + (v1 as u64) / 16) as u8 && ((x >> 48) & 255) as u8 == ((v1 as u64) % 16 * 16 + (v2 as u64) / 4) as u8 && ((x >> 40) & 255) as u8 == ((v2 as u64) % 4 * 64 + (v3 as u64)) as u8 && ((x >> 32) & 255) as u8 == ((v4 as u64) * 4 + (v5 as u64) / 16) as u8 && ((x >> 24) & 255) as u8 == ((v5 as u64) % 16 * 16 + (v6 as u64) / 4) as u8 && ((x >> 16) & 255) as u8 == ((v6 as u64) % 4 * 64 + (v7 as u64)) as u8 },
+{
+    assert({ let x: u64 = ((v0 as u64) << 58) | ((v1 as u64) << 52) | ((v2 as u64) << 46) | ((v3 as u64) << 40) | ((v4 as u64) << 34) | ((v5 as u64) << 28) | ((v6 as u64) << 22) | ((v7 as u64) << 16); ((x >> 56) & 255) as u8 == ((v0 as u64) * 4 + (v1 as u64) / 16) as u8 && ((x >> 48) & 255) as u8 == ((v1 as u64) % 16 * 16 + (v2 as u64) / 4) as u8 && ((x >> 40) & 255) as u8 == ((v2 as u64) % 4 * 64 + (v3 as u64)) as u8 && ((x >> 32) & 255) as u8 == ((v4 as u64) * 4 + (v5 as u64) / 16) as u8 && ((x >> 24) & 255) as u8 == ((v5 as u64) % 16 * 16 + (v6 as u64) / 4) as u8 && ((x >> 16) & 255) as u8 == ((v6 as u64) % 4 * 64 + (v7 as u64)) as u8 }) by(bit_vector)
+        requires v0 < 64, v1 < 64, v2 < 64, v3 < 64, v4 < 64, v5 < 64, v6 < 64, v7 < 64;
+}
+pub open spec fn table_pack64(input: Seq<u8>,table: Seq<u8>) -> u64 {
+    ((table[input[0] as int] as u64) << 58) | ((table[input[1] as int] as u64) << 52) | ((table[input[2] as int] as u64) << 46) | ((table[input[3] as int] as u64) << 40) | ((table[input[4] as int] as u64) << 34) | ((table[input[5] as int] as u64) << 28) | ((table[input[6] as int] as u64) << 22) | ((table[input[7] as int] as u64) << 16)
+}
+pub proof fn table_unpack64(input: Seq<u8>,table: Seq<u8>,x: u64)
+    requires super::decode::quad_valid(input,table,0,8), x == table_pack64(input,table),
+    ensures ((x >> 56) & 255) as u8 == super::decode::quad_byte(input,table,0), ((x >> 48) & 255) as u8 == super::decode::quad_byte(input,table,1), ((x >> 40) & 255) as u8 == super::decode::quad_byte(input,table,2), ((x >> 32) & 255) as u8 == super::decode::quad_byte(input,table,3), ((x >> 24) & 255) as u8 == super::decode::quad_byte(input,table,4), ((x >> 16) & 255) as u8 == super::decode::quad_byte(input,table,5),
+{
+    reveal(super::decode::quad_byte);
+    sextet_unpack64(table[input[0] as int], table[input[1] as int], table[input[2] as int], table[input[3] as int], table[input[4] as int], table[input[5] as int], table[input[6] as int], table[input[7] as int]);
+}
+
+pub proof fn image_recovery(input: Seq<u8>,table: Seq<u8>,b: Seq<u8>)
+    requires (b.len() == 3 || b.len() == 6), inverse(table), image(input,b),
+    ensures forall|j: int| 0 <= j < b.len() ==> super::decode::quad_byte(input,table,j) == b[j],
+{
+    image_sextets(input,table,b);
+    if b.len() == 3 {
+        assert(super::decode::quad_valid(input,table,0,4));
+        assert(table_pack32(input,table) == packed32(b));
+        recover32(b,packed32(b));
+        table_unpack32(input,table,packed32(b));
+    } else {
+        assert(super::decode::quad_valid(input,table,0,8));
+        assert(table_pack64(input,table) == packed64(b));
+        recover64(b,packed64(b));
+        table_unpack64(input,table,packed64(b));
+    }
+    assert forall|j: int| 0 <= j < b.len() implies super::decode::quad_byte(input,table,j) == b[j] by {
+        if j == 0 {} else if j == 1 {} else if j == 2 {}
+        else if j == 3 {} else if j == 4 {} else if j == 5 {}
+    }
+}
+

+verus! {
+// t27 meaning: table values are sextets on the selected nonterminal segment.
+pub open spec fn quad_valid(input: Seq<u8>, table: Seq<u8>, lo: int, hi: int) -> bool {
+    0 <= lo <= hi <= input.len() && table.len() == 256 &&
+    forall|i: int| lo <= i < hi ==> table[input[i] as int] < 64
+}
+#[verifier::opaque]
+pub open spec fn quad_byte(input: Seq<u8>, table: Seq<u8>, j: int) -> u8 {
+    let q = j / 3 * 4;
+    let a = table[input[q] as int] as int;
+    let b = table[input[q+1] as int] as int;
+    let c = table[input[q+2] as int] as int;
+    let d = table[input[q+3] as int] as int;
+    if j % 3 == 0 { (a * 4 + b / 16) as u8 }
+    else if j % 3 == 1 { ((b % 16) * 16 + c / 4) as u8 }
+    else { ((c % 4) * 64 + d) as u8 }
+}
+pub proof fn quad_subrange(input: Seq<u8>, table: Seq<u8>, lo: int, hi: int)
+    requires 0 <= lo <= hi <= input.len(), lo % 4 == 0,
+    ensures forall|j: int| 0 <= j < (hi - lo) / 4 * 3 ==>
+        quad_byte(input.subrange(lo, hi), table, j) == quad_byte(input, table, lo / 4 * 3 + j),
+{
+    assert forall|j: int| 0 <= j < (hi - lo) / 4 * 3 implies
+        quad_byte(input.subrange(lo, hi), table, j) == quad_byte(input, table, lo / 4 * 3 + j) by {
+        reveal(quad_byte);
+        assert((lo / 4 * 3 + j) / 3 == lo / 4 + j / 3) by (nonlinear_arith)
+            requires lo % 4 == 0;
+        assert((lo / 4 * 3 + j) % 3 == j % 3) by (nonlinear_arith);
+    }
+}
+
+// The semantic connection to canonical STANDARD input. This is a checked lemma,
+// not an assumption that malformed input is canonical.
+pub proof fn canonical_quad_byte(b: Seq<u8>, table: Seq<u8>, j: int)
+    requires table.len() == 256, 0 <= j, j / 3 * 3 + 2 < b.len(),
+        forall|v: int| 0 <= v < 64 ==> table[crate::round_trip_spec::symbol(v) as int] == v,
+    ensures quad_byte(crate::round_trip_spec::encoding(b), table, j) == b[j],
+        forall|k: int| j / 3 * 4 <= k < j / 3 * 4 + 4 ==>
+            table[crate::round_trip_spec::encoding(b)[k] as int] < 64,
+{
+    let q = j / 3 * 3;
+    let a = b[q] as int;
+    let c = b[q+1] as int;
+    let d = b[q+2] as int;
+    let enc = crate::round_trip_spec::encoding(b);
+    assert(enc[j/3*4] == crate::round_trip_spec::symbol(a/4));
+    assert(enc[j/3*4+1] == crate::round_trip_spec::symbol((a%4)*16+c/16));
+    assert(enc[j/3*4+2] == crate::round_trip_spec::symbol((c%16)*4+d/64));
+    assert(enc[j/3*4+3] == crate::round_trip_spec::symbol(d%64));
+    assert(0 <= (a%4)*16+c/16 < 64);
+    assert(0 <= (c%16)*4+d/64 < 64);
+    assert(table[enc[j/3*4] as int] == a/4);
+    assert(table[enc[j/3*4+1] as int] == (a%4)*16+c/16);
+    assert(table[enc[j/3*4+2] as int] == (c%16)*4+d/64);
+    assert(table[enc[j/3*4+3] as int] == d%64);
+    reveal(quad_byte);
+}
+
+// Checked consumption over the encoder image. The actual public decoder/helper
+// remains a separate caller obligation; this function adds no runtime callback.
+pub(crate) fn canonical_complete_quads_use(
+    input: &[u8], table: &[u8; 256], output: &mut [u8],
+    start: usize, end: usize, Ghost(b): Ghost<Seq<u8>>,
+) -> (r: Result<(), DecodeSliceError>)
+    requires input@ == crate::round_trip_spec::encoding(b),
+        start <= end <= input.len(), start % 4 == 0, end % 4 == 0,
+        end as int / 4 * 3 <= b.len(),
+        old(output).len() >= end as int / 4 * 3,
+        forall|v: int| 0 <= v < 64 ==> table@[crate::round_trip_spec::symbol(v) as int] == v,
+    ensures r is Ok, final(output).len() == old(output).len(),
+        forall|j: int| start as int / 4 * 3 <= j < end as int / 4 * 3 ==>
+            final(output)@[j] == b[j],
+        forall|j: int| 0 <= j < final(output).len() &&
+            (j < start as int / 4 * 3 || end as int / 4 * 3 <= j)
+            ==> final(output)@[j] == old(output)@[j],
+{
+    proof {
+        assert forall|i: int| start <= i < end implies table@[input@[i] as int] < 64 by {
+            canonical_quad_byte(b, table@, i / 4 * 3);
+        }
+        assert(quad_valid(input@, table@, start as int, end as int));
+    }
+    let r = decode_complete_quads(input, start, end, table, output, start / 4 * 3);
+    proof {
+        assert forall|j: int| start as int / 4 * 3 <= j < end as int / 4 * 3 implies
+            output@[j] == b[j] by {
+            canonical_quad_byte(b, table@, j);
+        }
+    }
+    r
+}

 /// Decode the complete non-terminal quads in `input[input_index_start..input_index_end]` (both
 /// bounds multiples of 4), writing to `output` starting at `output_index_start`, which must equal
 /// `input_index_start / 4 * 3`. Error offsets are reported in absolute input coordinates.

+            image_to_suffix(b,input@,decode_table@,input_index as int,before.len() as int);
+            assert forall|j:int| initial_output_index <= j < b.len() implies output@[j] == b[j] by {
+                assert(recovered(input@,input_index as int,decode_table@,j-initial_output_index) == b[j]);
+            }
+        }
+    }
+    result
+}
+

+verus! {
+// Concrete T33 readiness + accepted T26 boundary feed the actual suffix.
+// This consumer preserves any prior decoded prefix; it does not produce that prefix.
+pub(crate) fn standard_suffix_use(input: &[u8], output: &mut [u8], Ghost(b): Ghost<Seq<u8>>)
+    -> (dm: DecodeMetadata)
+    requires crate::decoder_contract::image(b,input@),
+        old(output).len() >= crate::round_trip_spec::decoded_capacity(input.len() as int),
+    ensures crate::decoder_contract::metadata(b,Ok(dm)),
+        crate::decoder_contract::range_write(old(output)@,final(output)@,b,
+            crate::decoder_contract::boundary(input.len() as int)/4*3,b.len() as int),
+{
+    let e = super::STANDARD;
+    proof {
+        e.decoder_readiness();
+        crate::decoder_contract::image_bounds(b,input@);
+    }
+    let start = super::decode::complete_quads_len(input,input.len()%4,output.len(),
+        &e.decode_table,e.padding).unwrap();
+    let output_start = start / 4 * 3;
+    let result = decode_suffix(input,start,output,output_start,&e.decode_table,
+        e.config.decode_allow_trailing_bits,e.padding,e.config.decode_padding_mode);
+    result.unwrap()
+}

+impl Alphabet {
+    /// T22 premise, to be produced for the concrete STANDARD alphabet by T21.
+    pub closed spec fn standard_symbols(&self) -> bool {
+        self.padding_symbol().byte() == 61u8
+        && forall|v: int| 0 <= v < 64 ==> self.symbols@[v] == crate::round_trip_spec::symbol(v)
+    }
+    /// Introduction rule for the T21 producer from the actual alphabet fields.
+    pub(crate) proof fn standard_symbols_intro(&self)
+        requires self.padding_symbol().byte() == 61u8,
+            forall|v: int| 0 <= v < 64 ==> self.symbols@[v] == crate::round_trip_spec::symbol(v),
+        ensures self.standard_symbols(),
+    {}
+    /// Conditional distinctness/exclusion over the actual alphabet storage.
+    pub(crate) proof fn standard_symbols_distinct(&self, v: int, w: int)
+        requires self.standard_symbols(), 0 <= v < 64, 0 <= w < 64, v != w,
+        ensures self.symbols@[v] != self.symbols@[w],
+            self.symbols@[v] != self.padding_symbol().byte(),
+    { standard_symbol_separation(v, w); }
+}
+impl GeneralPurpose {
+    /// Decoder configuration observations project the stored configuration.
+    pub closed spec fn decoder_padding_mode(&self) -> DecodePaddingMode { self.config.decode_padding_mode }
+    /// Whether the stored decoder configuration permits unused trailing bits.
+    pub closed spec fn decoder_allows_trailing_bits(&self) -> bool { self.config.decode_allow_trailing_bits }
+    /// Bridge to the predicates consumed by the actual decoder implementation.
+    pub proof fn decoder_readiness(&self)
+        requires self.standard_decode_inverse(), self.padding_symbol().byte() == 61u8,
+            self.decoder_padding_mode() == DecodePaddingMode::RequireCanonical,
+            !self.decoder_allows_trailing_bits(),
+        ensures self.canonical_decoder(),
+    {}
+    /// Physical decode-table observation.
+    pub closed spec fn decode_value(&self, byte: u8) -> u8 { self.decode_table@[byte as int] }
+    /// Canonical six-bit inverse, with padding excluded from data.
+    pub open spec fn standard_decode_inverse(&self) -> bool {
+        (forall|v: int| 0 <= v < 64 ==>
+            self.decode_value(crate::round_trip_spec::symbol(v)) == v as u8)
+        && self.decode_value(61u8) == 255u8
+    }
+}
+/// Canonical symbols are injective and exclude the padding byte.
+pub proof fn standard_symbol_separation(v: int, w: int)
+    requires 0 <= v < 64, 0 <= w < 64,
+    ensures crate::round_trip_spec::symbol(v) != 61u8,
+        (crate::round_trip_spec::symbol(v) == crate::round_trip_spec::symbol(w) ==> v == w),
+{}

+//! Explicit standard-library models for the unchanged read_u64 wrapper.
+use vstd::prelude::*;
+verus! {
+/// Big-endian shift/OR value of the first eight bytes.
+pub open spec fn be_word(s: Seq<u8>) -> u64 {
+    ((s[0] as u64) << 56u64) | ((s[1] as u64) << 48u64)
+    | ((s[2] as u64) << 40u64) | ((s[3] as u64) << 32u64)
+    | ((s[4] as u64) << 24u64) | ((s[5] as u64) << 16u64)
+    | ((s[6] as u64) << 8u64) | (s[7] as u64)
+}
+/// Standard u64 big-endian byte-array conversion interface.
+#[verifier::external_fn_specification]
+pub fn ex_from_be_bytes(s: [u8; 8]) -> (r: u64)
+    ensures r == be_word(s@),
+{ u64::from_be_bytes(s) }
+}
