+//! Internal proof interfaces. Every opaque item here is a named unfinished obligation.
+use vstd::prelude::*;
+verus! {
+// P-SIZES arithmetic from t1/check-open-spec.rs, now consumed by the actual helper.
+pub proof fn sizes(n: usize)
+    requires crate::round_trip_spec::supported(n as int),
+    ensures
+        0 <= crate::round_trip_spec::encoded_size(n as int) <= isize::MAX as int,
+        n as int <= crate::round_trip_spec::decoded_capacity(
+            crate::round_trip_spec::encoded_size(n as int)) <= isize::MAX as int,
+        crate::round_trip_spec::encoded_size(n as int) % 4 == 0,
+{}
+
+// Exact unpadded Base64 count; the GeneralPurpose producer is checked in t18,
+// conditional on the explicit unfinished read_u64 leaf and adaptation correspondence.
+pub open spec fn unpadded_size(n: int) -> int {
+    4 * (n / 3) + if n % 3 == 0 { 0 } else { n % 3 + 1 }
+}
+pub open spec fn padding_count(k: int) -> int { (4 - k % 4) % 4 }
+
+pub proof fn padding_sizes(n: usize, k: usize)
+    requires crate::round_trip_spec::supported(n as int),
+        k as int == unpadded_size(n as int),
+    ensures
+        0 <= k <= crate::round_trip_spec::encoded_size(n as int),
+        0 <= padding_count(k as int) <= 2,
+        k + padding_count(k as int) == crate::round_trip_spec::encoded_size(n as int),
+        k + padding_count(k as int) <= usize::MAX,
+{
+    sizes(n);
+}
+
+// The canonical trailing positions are '='; no premise about internal encoder bytes.
+pub proof fn canonical_padding(b: Seq<u8>, k: usize)
+    requires crate::round_trip_spec::supported(b.len() as int),
+        k as int == unpadded_size(b.len() as int),
+    ensures forall|j: int| k <= j < crate::round_trip_spec::encoded_size(b.len() as int)
+        ==> crate::round_trip_spec::encoded_byte(b, j) == 61u8,
+{
+    assert forall|j: int| k <= j < crate::round_trip_spec::encoded_size(b.len() as int)
+        implies crate::round_trip_spec::encoded_byte(b, j) == 61u8 by {
+    }
+}
+
+// Always callable at the public path; exports only a conditional consequence.
+// This does not claim that the executed internal encoder establishes the antecedent.
+pub proof fn padding_sizes_if(n: usize, k: usize)
+    ensures (n <= isize::MAX
+        && k as int == unpadded_size(n as int)) ==>
+        (0 <= k <= crate::round_trip_spec::encoded_size(n as int)
+        && 0 <= padding_count(k as int) <= 2
+        && k + padding_count(k as int) == crate::round_trip_spec::encoded_size(n as int)
+        && k + padding_count(k as int) <= usize::MAX),
+{
+    if n <= isize::MAX && k as int == unpadded_size(n as int) {
+    }
+}
+
+// T52-RUST-BYTE-SLICE-VALIDITY (new explicit library/unsafe-validity TCB).
+// Rust Reference wide-reference validity forbids pointed-to size > isize::MAX;
+// slice::from_raw_parts likewise requires this bound. For u8, size_of is one. This
+// interface applies only to actual valid &[u8], including valid unsafe-created
+// slices, never arbitrary Seq<u8>, usize lengths, or hypothetical allocations.
+// vstd currently exports only the weaker usize bound. Independent review owed.
+#[verifier::external_body]
+pub proof fn byte_slice_size_bound(bytes: &[u8])
+    ensures bytes@.len() <= isize::MAX as int,
+{}
+
+pub proof fn byte_slice_encoding_fits(n: usize, padding: bool)
+    requires n <= isize::MAX,
+    ensures crate::round_trip_spec::encoding_size(n as int, padding) <= usize::MAX as int,
+{}
+
+// A physical padded destination can hold only supported source lengths.
+pub proof fn padded_capacity_supported(n: usize, capacity: usize)
+    requires capacity <= isize::MAX,
+        capacity as int >= crate::round_trip_spec::encoded_size(n as int),
+    ensures crate::round_trip_spec::supported(n as int),
+{}
+
+// Encoding table meaning is now a type-owned predicate; STANDARD obtains it
+// from the accepted concrete T21 table witness (not an uninterpreted capability).
+pub open spec fn is_standard<E: crate::engine::Engine + ?Sized>(engine: &E) -> bool {
+    engine.encoding_padding() && engine.canonical_encoding_table() && engine.padding_symbol().byte() == 61u8
+}
+// P-GENERIC-INTERFACE: translation only. No relation to a stable pure View,
+// no totality/no-panic claim, no interior-effect or Drop/frame guarantee.
+#[verifier::external_trait_specification]
+pub trait ExAsRef<T: core::marker::PointeeSized>: core::marker::PointeeSized {
+    type ExternalTraitSpecificationFor: core::convert::AsRef<T>;
+    fn as_ref(&self) -> &T;
+}
+// P-STD-ASREF: exact core implementations, assumed library interfaces only.
+// The slice implementation returns self; the reference implementation delegates once.
+#[verifier::external_fn_specification]
+pub fn slice_as_ref<T>(s: &[T]) -> (r: &[T])
+    ensures r@ == s@
+{ <[T] as AsRef<[T]>>::as_ref(s) }
+#[verifier::external_fn_specification]
+pub fn reference_as_ref<'a, 'b,
+    T: core::marker::PointeeSized + AsRef<U>, U: core::marker::PointeeSized>
+    (s: &'b &'a T) -> (r: &'b U)
+    ensures call_ensures(<T as AsRef<U>>::as_ref, (*s,), r)
+{ <&'a T as AsRef<U>>::as_ref(s) }
+
+// P-STD-ASSERT-FAILED: core panic path; require it unreachable rather than
+// granting any successful effect. The runtime function and formatting remain intact.
+#[verifier::external_type_specification]
+pub struct ExAssertKind(core::panicking::AssertKind);
+#[verifier::external_fn_specification]
+pub fn ex_assert_failed<T: core::fmt::Debug + ?Sized, U: core::fmt::Debug + ?Sized>(
+        kind: core::panicking::AssertKind, left: &T, right: &U,
+        args: Option<core::fmt::Arguments<'_>>,
+    ) -> !
+    requires false
+{ core::panicking::assert_failed(kind, left, right, args) }
+pub proof fn masked_u64(x: u64)
+    ensures
+        (x >> 58u64) & 63u64 < 64,
+        (x >> 52u64) & 63u64 < 64,
+        (x >> 46u64) & 63u64 < 64,
+        (x >> 40u64) & 63u64 < 64,
+        (x >> 34u64) & 63u64 < 64,
+        (x >> 28u64) & 63u64 < 64,
+        (x >> 22u64) & 63u64 < 64,
+        (x >> 16u64) & 63u64 < 64,
+{ assert((x >> 58u64) & 63u64 < 64 && (x >> 52u64) & 63u64 < 64 && (x >> 46u64) & 63u64 < 64 && (x >> 40u64) & 63u64 < 64 && (x >> 34u64) & 63u64 < 64 && (x >> 28u64) & 63u64 < 64 && (x >> 22u64) & 63u64 < 64 && (x >> 16u64) & 63u64 < 64) by(bit_vector); }
+pub proof fn byte_indices(a: u8, b: u8, c: u8)
+    ensures
+        a >> 2u8 < 64,
+        ((a << 4u8 | b >> 4u8) & 63u8) < 64,
+        ((b << 2u8 | c >> 6u8) & 63u8) < 64,
+        c & 63u8 < 64,
+        (a << 4u8) & 63u8 < 64,
+        (b << 2u8) & 63u8 < 64,
+{ assert(a >> 2u8 < 64 && ((a << 4u8 | b >> 4u8) & 63u8) < 64 && ((b << 2u8 | c >> 6u8) & 63u8) < 64 && c & 63u8 < 64 && (a << 4u8) & 63u8 < 64 && (b << 2u8) & 63u8 < 64) by(bit_vector); }
+}

+//! Open, caller-visible meaning for the STANDARD slice round trip.
+//! No executable codec and no trusted operation bodies live here.
+#![allow(missing_docs)]
+use vstd::prelude::*;
+verus! {
+pub open spec fn encoded_size(n: int) -> int { 4 * ((n + 2) / 3) }
+// Size for either lawful Engine padding choice; no global STANDARD premise.
+pub open spec fn encoding_size(n: int, padding: bool) -> int {
+    if padding { encoded_size(n) } else { 4 * (n / 3) + if n % 3 == 0 { 0 } else { n % 3 + 1 } }
+}
+// Timeless admitted-reply relation, NOT an invocation identity or pure View.
+// Public codec calls separately export their actual snapshot in an exclusive
+// erased event slot; only that captured snapshot indexes their guarantees.
+pub open spec fn observed<T: AsRef<[u8]>>(input: T, bytes: &[u8]) -> bool {
+    call_ensures(<T as AsRef<[u8]>>::as_ref, (&input,), bytes)
+}
+// Optional sufficient admission envelope for legacy callers; not a public
+// codec precondition and not required for actual-event lawfulness.
+pub open spec fn encode_domain<T: AsRef<[u8]>>(input: T) -> bool {
+    forall|bytes: &[u8]| #[trigger] observed(input, bytes) ==> supported(bytes@.len() as int)
+}
+pub open spec fn decoded_capacity(m: int) -> int { 3 * ((m + 3) / 4) }
+pub open spec fn supported(n: int) -> bool {
+    0 <= n <= 3 * ((isize::MAX as int) / 4)
+}
+pub open spec fn encoded_helper_post(n: int, r: Option<usize>) -> bool {
+    if encoded_size(n) <= usize::MAX as int {
+        r == Some(encoded_size(n) as usize)
+    } else { r == None }
+}
+pub open spec fn decoded_helper_post(m: int, r: usize) -> bool {
+    r as int == decoded_capacity(m)
+}
+pub open spec fn symbol(v: int) -> u8 {
+    if v < 26 { (65 + v) as u8 }
+    else if v < 52 { (97 + v - 26) as u8 }
+    else if v < 62 { (48 + v - 52) as u8 }
+    else if v == 62 { 43u8 } else { 47u8 }
+}
+pub open spec fn byte_or_zero(b: Seq<u8>, k: int) -> int {
+    if 0 <= k < b.len() { b[k] as int } else { 0 }
+}
+pub open spec fn encoded_byte(b: Seq<u8>, i: int) -> u8 {
+    let j = 3 * (i / 4);
+    let a = byte_or_zero(b, j);
+    let c = byte_or_zero(b, j + 1);
+    let d = byte_or_zero(b, j + 2);
+    if i % 4 == 0 { symbol(a / 4) }
+    else if i % 4 == 1 { symbol((a % 4) * 16 + c / 16) }
+    else if i % 4 == 2 {
+        if j + 1 >= b.len() { 61u8 } else { symbol((c % 16) * 4 + d / 64) }
+    } else {
+        if j + 2 >= b.len() { 61u8 } else { symbol(d % 64) }
+    }
+}
+pub open spec fn encoding(b: Seq<u8>) -> Seq<u8> {
+    Seq::new(encoded_size(b.len() as int) as nat, |i: int| encoded_byte(b, i))
+}
+pub open spec fn write_frame(before: Seq<u8>, after: Seq<u8>, prefix: Seq<u8>) -> bool {
+    &&& before.len() == after.len()
+    &&& prefix.len() <= after.len()
+    &&& after.take(prefix.len() as int) == prefix
+    &&& forall|i: int| prefix.len() <= i < after.len() ==> after[i] == before[i]
+}
+pub open spec fn encode_post(b: Seq<u8>, before: Seq<u8>, after: Seq<u8>, written: int) -> bool {
+    written == encoded_size(b.len() as int) && write_frame(before, after, encoding(b))
+}
+pub open spec fn canonical_input(input: Seq<u8>) -> bool {
+    exists|b: Seq<u8>| supported(b.len() as int) && encoding(b) == input
+}
+/// Optional sufficient admission envelope for legacy callers. Actual-event
+/// canonicality/capacity is sufficient even when unrelated admitted replies fail.
+pub open spec fn decode_domain<T: AsRef<[u8]>>(input: T, capacity: int) -> bool {
+    forall|bytes: &[u8]| #[trigger] observed(input, bytes)
+        ==> canonical_input(bytes@) && capacity >= decoded_capacity(bytes.len() as int)
+}
+/// Lawfulness of the one snapshot actually consumed by an invocation.
+/// These predicates say nothing about another conversion, admission, or context.
+pub open spec fn encode_event_lawful(bytes: Seq<u8>, capacity: int) -> bool {
+    supported(bytes.len() as int) && capacity >= encoded_size(bytes.len() as int)
+}
+pub open spec fn decode_event_lawful(bytes: Seq<u8>, capacity: int) -> bool {
+    canonical_input(bytes) && capacity >= decoded_capacity(bytes.len() as int)
+}
+pub open spec fn decode_post(b: Seq<u8>, before: Seq<u8>, after: Seq<u8>, written: int) -> bool {
+    written == b.len() && write_frame(before, after, b)
+}
+pub open spec fn round_trip_post(
+    b: Seq<u8>, b_after: Seq<u8>, encoded_before: Seq<u8>, encoded_after: Seq<u8>,
+    decoded_before: Seq<u8>, decoded_after: Seq<u8>, encode_ok: bool, decode_ok: bool,
+    encoded_written: int, decoded_written: int,
+) -> bool {
+    &&& b_after == b
+    &&& encode_ok && decode_ok
+    &&& encode_post(b, encoded_before, encoded_after, encoded_written)
+    &&& decode_post(b, decoded_before, decoded_after, decoded_written)
+}
+}

+//! Scalar content bridges, consumed by the actual encoder body.
+use vstd::prelude::*;
+use crate::round_trip_spec::{encoded_byte, symbol, byte_or_zero};
+verus! {
+pub open spec fn canonical_table(t: Seq<u8>) -> bool {
+    t.len() == 64 && forall|i: int| 0 <= i < 64 ==> #[trigger] t[i] == symbol(i)
+}
+#[verifier::opaque]
+pub open spec fn be_word(s: Seq<u8>) -> u64 {
+    ((s[0] as u64) << 56u64) | ((s[1] as u64) << 48u64)
+    | ((s[2] as u64) << 40u64) | ((s[3] as u64) << 32u64)
+    | ((s[4] as u64) << 24u64) | ((s[5] as u64) << 16u64)
+    | ((s[6] as u64) << 8u64) | (s[7] as u64)
+}
+pub proof fn byte_values(a: u8, b: u8, c: u8)
+    ensures
+        (a >> 2u8) as int == a as int / 4,
+        ((a << 4u8 | b >> 4u8) & 63u8) as int == (a as int % 4) * 16 + b as int / 16,
+        ((b << 2u8 | c >> 6u8) & 63u8) as int == (b as int % 16) * 4 + c as int / 64,
+        (c & 63u8) as int == c as int % 64,
+        ((a << 4u8) & 63u8) as int == (a as int % 4) * 16,
+        ((b << 2u8) & 63u8) as int == (b as int % 16) * 4,
+{
+    assert(a >> 2u8 == a / 4u8) by(bit_vector);
+    assert(((a << 4u8 | b >> 4u8) & 63u8) as u16 == (a as u16 % 4u16) * 16u16 + b as u16 / 16u16) by(bit_vector);
+    assert(((b << 2u8 | c >> 6u8) & 63u8) as u16 == (b as u16 % 16u16) * 4u16 + c as u16 / 64u16) by(bit_vector);
+    assert(c & 63u8 == c % 64u8) by(bit_vector);
+    assert(((a << 4u8) & 63u8) as u16 == (a as u16 % 4u16) * 16u16) by(bit_vector);
+    assert(((b << 2u8) & 63u8) as u16 == (b as u16 % 16u16) * 4u16) by(bit_vector);
+}
+pub proof fn word_values(s: Seq<u8>, x: u64)
+    requires s.len() >= 8, x == be_word(s),
+    ensures
+        ((x >> 58u64) & 63u64) as int == s[0] as int / 4,
+        ((x >> 52u64) & 63u64) as int == (s[0] as int % 4) * 16 + s[1] as int / 16,
+        ((x >> 46u64) & 63u64) as int == (s[1] as int % 16) * 4 + s[2] as int / 64,
+        ((x >> 40u64) & 63u64) as int == s[2] as int % 64,
+        ((x >> 34u64) & 63u64) as int == s[3] as int / 4,
+        ((x >> 28u64) & 63u64) as int == (s[3] as int % 4) * 16 + s[4] as int / 16,
+        ((x >> 22u64) & 63u64) as int == (s[4] as int % 16) * 4 + s[5] as int / 64,
+        ((x >> 16u64) & 63u64) as int == s[5] as int % 64,
+{
+    reveal(be_word);
+    let a=s[0]; let b=s[1]; let c=s[2]; let d=s[3];
+    let e=s[4]; let f=s[5]; let g=s[6]; let h=s[7];
+    assert(x == ((a as u64)<<56u64) | ((b as u64)<<48u64) | ((c as u64)<<40u64) | ((d as u64)<<32u64) | ((e as u64)<<24u64) | ((f as u64)<<16u64) | ((g as u64)<<8u64) | h as u64);
+    assert(
+        (x >> 58u64) & 63u64 == (a >> 2u8) as u64 &&
+        (x >> 52u64) & 63u64 == ((a << 4u8 | b >> 4u8) & 63u8) as u64 &&
+        (x >> 46u64) & 63u64 == ((b << 2u8 | c >> 6u8) & 63u8) as u64 &&
+        (x >> 40u64) & 63u64 == (c & 63u8) as u64 &&
+        (x >> 34u64) & 63u64 == (d >> 2u8) as u64 &&
+        (x >> 28u64) & 63u64 == ((d << 4u8 | e >> 4u8) & 63u8) as u64 &&
+        (x >> 22u64) & 63u64 == ((e << 2u8 | f >> 6u8) & 63u8) as u64 &&
+        (x >> 16u64) & 63u64 == (f & 63u8) as u64
+    ) by(bit_vector)
+      requires x == ((a as u64)<<56u64) | ((b as u64)<<48u64) | ((c as u64)<<40u64) | ((d as u64)<<32u64) | ((e as u64)<<24u64) | ((f as u64)<<16u64) | ((g as u64)<<8u64) | h as u64;
+    byte_values(a,b,c); byte_values(d,e,f);
+}
+pub proof fn group_meaning(b: Seq<u8>, k: int)
+    requires 0 <= k < b.len(), k % 3 == 0,
+    ensures
+        encoded_byte(b, 4*(k/3)) == symbol(byte_or_zero(b,k)/4),
+        encoded_byte(b, 4*(k/3)+1) == symbol((byte_or_zero(b,k)%4)*16+byte_or_zero(b,k+1)/16),
+        k+1 < b.len() ==> encoded_byte(b, 4*(k/3)+2) == symbol((byte_or_zero(b,k+1)%16)*4+byte_or_zero(b,k+2)/64),
+        k+2 < b.len() ==> encoded_byte(b, 4*(k/3)+3) == symbol(byte_or_zero(b,k+2)%64),
+{}
+// A checked consumer of the real scalar and accepted padding bodies. Table
+// meaning is an explicit producer premise; this is not the public API bridge.
+pub(crate) fn scalar_padding_use(table: &[u8; 64], input: &[u8], output: &mut [u8]) -> (r: usize)
+    requires crate::round_trip_spec::supported(input.len() as int),
+        canonical_table(table@),
+        old(output).len() >= crate::round_trip_spec::encoded_size(input.len() as int),
+    ensures crate::round_trip_spec::encode_post(input@, old(output)@, final(output)@, r as int),
+{
+    use crate::engine::Engine;
+    let engine = crate::engine::general_purpose::STANDARD;
+    let padding = engine.padding();
+    let size = crate::encode::encoded_len(input.len(), true).expect("usize overflow when calculating buffer size");
+    let bounded = &mut output[0..size];
+    let k = crate::engine::general_purpose::encode_scalar_tail(table, input, bounded, 0, 0);
+    proof {
+        crate::round_trip_proof::padding_sizes(input.len(), k);
+        crate::round_trip_proof::canonical_padding(input@, k);
+    }
+    let p = crate::encode::add_padding(k, padding, &mut bounded[k..]);
+    let r = k.checked_add(p).expect("usize overflow when calculating b64 length");
+    proof {
+        assert(bounded@.take(r as int) =~= crate::round_trip_spec::encoding(input@));
+        assert(output@.take(r as int) =~= crate::round_trip_spec::encoding(input@));
+        assert forall|j: int| r <= j < output.len() implies output@[j] == old(output)@[j] by {}
+    }
+    r
+}

+//! Canonical terminal image facts consumed by the real suffix decoder.
+#![allow(missing_docs)]
+use vstd::prelude::*;
+use crate::round_trip_spec::*;
+verus! {
+pub open spec fn symbol_count(input: Seq<u8>, start: int) -> int {
+    if input.len() == start { 0 }
+    else if input[start + 2] == 61 { 2 }
+    else if input[start + 3] == 61 { 3 } else { 4 }
+}
+pub open spec fn digit(input: Seq<u8>, start: int, table: Seq<u8>, j: int) -> u8 {
+    if j < symbol_count(input, start) { table[input[start + j] as int] } else { 0 }
+}
+pub open spec fn packed(input: Seq<u8>, start: int, table: Seq<u8>) -> u32 {
+    ((digit(input,start,table,0) as u32) << 26)
+    | ((digit(input,start,table,1) as u32) << 20)
+    | ((digit(input,start,table,2) as u32) << 14)
+    | ((digit(input,start,table,3) as u32) << 8)
+}
+pub open spec fn append_count(input: Seq<u8>, start: int) -> int {
+    symbol_count(input,start) * 6 / 8
+}
+pub open spec fn recovered(input: Seq<u8>, start: int, table: Seq<u8>, j: int) -> u8 {
+    ((packed(input,start,table) << (j * 8)) >> 24) as u8
+}
+// Branch facts for the actual suffix consumer, established from the open image below.
+pub open spec fn canonical_suffix(input: Seq<u8>, start: int, table: Seq<u8>) -> bool {
+    &&& table.len() == 256
+    &&& 0 <= start <= input.len()
+    &&& start % 4 == 0
+    &&& (input.len() == 0 || input.len() - start == 4)
+    &&& forall|j: int| 0 <= j < input.len()-start ==>
+        if j < symbol_count(input,start) {
+            #[trigger] #[trigger] input[start+j] != 61 && table[input[start+j] as int] < 64
+        } else { input[start+j] == 61 }
+    &&& (packed(input,start,table) & (!0u32 >> (append_count(input,start)*8))) == 0
+}
+
+pub open spec fn terminal_len(b: Seq<u8>) -> int {
+    if b.len() == 0 { 0 } else { (b.len() - 1) % 3 + 1 }
+}
+pub open spec fn value(b: Seq<u8>, base: int, j: int) -> int {
+    let a = byte_or_zero(b,base);
+    let c = byte_or_zero(b,base+1);
+    let d = byte_or_zero(b,base+2);
+    if j == 0 { a/4 }
+    else if j == 1 { a%4*16+c/16 }
+    else if j == 2 { c%16*4+d/64 }
+    else { d%64 }
+}
+pub proof fn symbol_nonpadding(v: int)
+    requires 0 <= v < 64,
+    ensures symbol(v) != 61,
+{}
+
+// The shifts, ORs, mask and high-byte emissions are the actual suffix expressions.
+pub proof fn pack_bytes(a: u8, c: u8, d: u8)
+    ensures
+        (((a / 4u8) as u32) << 26
+         | (((a % 4u8) * 16 + (c / 16u8)) as u32) << 20
+         | (((c % 16u8) * 4 + (d / 64u8)) as u32) << 14
+         | ((d % 64u8) as u32) << 8)
+        == ((a as u32) << 24 | (c as u32) << 16 | (d as u32) << 8),
+{
+    assert((((a / 4u8) as u32) << 26
+         | (((a % 4u8) * 16 + (c / 16u8)) as u32) << 20
+         | (((c % 16u8) * 4 + (d / 64u8)) as u32) << 14
+         | ((d % 64u8) as u32) << 8)
+        == ((a as u32) << 24 | (c as u32) << 16 | (d as u32) << 8)) by(bit_vector);
+}
+pub proof fn shift_zero(x:u32)
+    ensures (x << 0) == x,
+{ assert((x << 0) == x) by(bit_vector); }
+pub proof fn packed_emissions(a: u8, c: u8, d: u8)
+    ensures
+        (((a as u32) << 24 | (c as u32) << 16 | (d as u32) << 8) >> 24) as u8 == a,
+        ((((a as u32) << 24 | (c as u32) << 16 | (d as u32) << 8) << 8) >> 24) as u8 == c,
+        ((((a as u32) << 24 | (c as u32) << 16 | (d as u32) << 8) << 16) >> 24) as u8 == d,
+        (((a as u32) << 24 | (c as u32) << 16 | (d as u32) << 8) & (!0u32 >> 24)) == 0,
+        c == 0 && d == 0 ==> (((a as u32) << 24 | (c as u32) << 16 | (d as u32) << 8) & (!0u32 >> 8)) == 0,
+        d == 0 ==> (((a as u32) << 24 | (c as u32) << 16 | (d as u32) << 8) & (!0u32 >> 16)) == 0,
+{
+    assert((((a as u32) << 24 | (c as u32) << 16 | (d as u32) << 8) >> 24) as u8 == a) by(bit_vector);
+    assert(((((a as u32) << 24 | (c as u32) << 16 | (d as u32) << 8) << 8) >> 24) as u8 == c) by(bit_vector);
+    assert(((((a as u32) << 24 | (c as u32) << 16 | (d as u32) << 8) << 16) >> 24) as u8 == d) by(bit_vector);
+    assert((((a as u32) << 24 | (c as u32) << 16 | (d as u32) << 8) & (!0u32 >> 24)) == 0) by(bit_vector);
+    assert(c == 0 && d == 0 ==> (((a as u32) << 24 | (c as u32) << 16 | (d as u32) << 8) & (!0u32 >> 8)) == 0) by(bit_vector);
+    assert(d == 0 ==> (((a as u32) << 24 | (c as u32) << 16 | (d as u32) << 8) & (!0u32 >> 16)) == 0) by(bit_vector);
+}
+
+// T22's checked conditional table constructor supplies table_ok. This theorem
+// reasons about the mathematical image; it does not assert encoder production.
+pub proof fn image_to_suffix(b: Seq<u8>, input: Seq<u8>, table: Seq<u8>, start: int, cap: int)
+    requires crate::decoder_contract::image(b,input), crate::decoder_contract::table_ok(table),
+        start == crate::decoder_contract::boundary(input.len() as int),
+        cap >= decoded_capacity(input.len() as int),
+    ensures
+        canonical_suffix(input,start,table),
+        append_count(input,start) == terminal_len(b),
+        start / 4 * 3 + append_count(input,start) == b.len(),
+        0 <= start / 4 * 3 <= b.len() <= cap,
+        append_count(input,start) <= cap - start / 4 * 3,
+        input.len() == 0 ==> symbol_count(input,start) == 0,
+        input.len() > 0 ==> 2 <= symbol_count(input,start) <= 4,
+        symbol_count(input,start) == if b.len() == 0 { 0 } else { terminal_len(b)+1 },
+        symbol_count(input,start) == 2 ==> digit(input,start,table,1) % 16 == 0,
+        symbol_count(input,start) == 3 ==> digit(input,start,table,2) % 4 == 0,
+        input.len() - start - symbol_count(input,start) ==
+            if b.len() % 3 == 0 { 0 } else { 3 - b.len() % 3 },
+        symbol_count(input,start) < input.len()-start ==>
+            start + symbol_count(input,start) == crate::round_trip_proof::unpadded_size(b.len() as int),
+        forall|j:int| 0 <= j < 4 ==> digit(input,start,table,j) ==
+            if j < symbol_count(input,start) { value(b,start/4*3,j) as u8 } else { 0u8 },
+        packed(input,start,table) ==
+            ((byte_or_zero(b,start/4*3) as u32) << 24
+             | (byte_or_zero(b,start/4*3+1) as u32) << 16
+             | (byte_or_zero(b,start/4*3+2) as u32) << 8),
+        forall|j:int| 0 <= j < append_count(input,start) ==>
+            recovered(input,start,table,j) == b[start/4*3+j],
+{
+    crate::decoder_contract::image_bounds(b,input);
+    let base = start/4*3;
+    let a = byte_or_zero(b,base) as u8;
+    let c = byte_or_zero(b,base+1) as u8;
+    let d = byte_or_zero(b,base+2) as u8;
+    assert(0 <= byte_or_zero(b,base) <= 255);
+    assert(0 <= byte_or_zero(b,base+1) <= 255);
+    assert(0 <= byte_or_zero(b,base+2) <= 255);
+    assert(terminal_len(b) == b.len() - base);
+    if b.len() > 0 {
+        assert(input.len()-start == 4);
+        assert(input[start] == symbol(value(b,base,0)));
+        assert(input[start+1] == symbol(value(b,base,1)));
+        symbol_nonpadding(value(b,base,0));
+        symbol_nonpadding(value(b,base,1));
+        if terminal_len(b) >= 2 {
+            assert(input[start+2] == symbol(value(b,base,2)));
+            symbol_nonpadding(value(b,base,2));
+        }
+        if terminal_len(b) == 3 {
+            assert(input[start+3] == symbol(value(b,base,3)));
+            symbol_nonpadding(value(b,base,3));
+        }
+    }
+    assert(symbol_count(input,start) == if b.len() == 0 { 0 } else { terminal_len(b)+1 });
+    assert forall|j:int| 0 <= j < input.len()-start implies
+        if j < symbol_count(input,start) {
+            #[trigger] input[start+j] != 61 && table[input[start+j] as int] < 64
+        } else { input[start+j] == 61 } by {
+        if j < symbol_count(input,start) {
+            assert(input[start+j] == symbol(value(b,base,j)));
+            assert(0 <= value(b,base,j) < 64);
+            symbol_nonpadding(value(b,base,j));
+            assert(table[input[start+j] as int] == value(b,base,j));
+        }
+    }
+    assert forall|j:int| 0 <= j < 4 implies #[trigger] digit(input,start,table,j) ==
+        if j < symbol_count(input,start) { value(b,base,j) as u8 } else { 0u8 } by {
+        if j < symbol_count(input,start) {
+            assert(input[start+j] == symbol(value(b,base,j)));
+            assert(0 <= value(b,base,j) < 64);
+            assert(table[input[start+j] as int] == value(b,base,j));
+        }
+    }
+    assert(digit(input,start,table,0) as int == a/4);
+    assert(digit(input,start,table,1) as int == a%4*16+c/16);
+    assert(digit(input,start,table,2) as int == c%16*4+d/64);
+    assert(digit(input,start,table,3) as int == d%64);
+    pack_bytes(a,c,d);
+    packed_emissions(a,c,d);
+    assert(append_count(input,start) == terminal_len(b));
+    assert(packed(input,start,table) == ((a as u32) << 24 | (c as u32) << 16 | (d as u32) << 8));
+    if b.len() == 0 {
+        assert(((0u32 << 24 | 0u32 << 16 | 0u32 << 8) & (!0u32 >> 0)) == 0) by(bit_vector);
+    } else if terminal_len(b) == 1 { assert(c == 0 && d == 0); }
+      else if terminal_len(b) == 2 { assert(d == 0); }
+      else { assert(terminal_len(b) == 3); }
+    assert((packed(input,start,table) & (!0u32 >> (append_count(input,start)*8))) == 0);
+    assert(canonical_suffix(input,start,table));
+    assert forall|j:int| 0 <= j < append_count(input,start) implies
+        recovered(input,start,table,j) == b[base+j] by {
+        reveal(recovered);
+        assert(packed(input,start,table) == ((a as u32) << 24 | (c as u32) << 16 | (d as u32) << 8));
+        if j == 0 {
+            assert(b[base] == a);
+            shift_zero(packed(input,start,table));
+            assert(recovered(input,start,table,j) == a);
+        }
+        else if j == 1 { assert(b[base+1] == c); assert(recovered(input,start,table,j) == c); }
+        else { assert(j == 2); assert(b[base+2] == d); assert(recovered(input,start,table,j) == d); }
+    }
+}
+
+// Checked source-bound usage of T26's actual boundary function. It exports the
+// image facts at the returned runtime offset, for T34's real suffix branches.
+// This helper does not decode or assert an actual encoder produced the image.
+pub(crate) fn canonical_terminal_use(
+    input: &[u8], table: &[u8;256], padding: crate::alphabet::Symbol,
+    cap: usize, Ghost(b): Ghost<Seq<u8>>,
+) -> (start: usize)
+    requires crate::decoder_contract::image(b,input@),
+        crate::decoder_contract::table_ok(table@), padding.byte() == 61,
+        cap >= decoded_capacity(input.len() as int),
+    ensures start as int == crate::decoder_contract::boundary(input.len() as int),
+        canonical_suffix(input@,start as int,table@),
+        start / 4 * 3 + append_count(input@,start as int) == b.len(),
+        append_count(input@,start as int) <= cap - start as int / 4 * 3,
+        symbol_count(input@,start as int) < input.len()-start ==>
+            start + symbol_count(input@,start as int) == crate::round_trip_proof::unpadded_size(b.len() as int),
+        forall|j:int| 0 <= j < append_count(input@,start as int) ==>
+            recovered(input@,start as int,table@,j) == b[start as int/4*3+j],
+{
+    let rem = input.len() % 4;
+    let r = crate::engine::general_purpose::decode::complete_quads_len(input,rem,cap,table,padding);
+    let start = r.unwrap();
+    proof { image_to_suffix(b,input@,table@,start as int,cap as int); }
+    start
+}
+}

+/// Run the eager initializer checks after Verus has initialized THIR erasure.
+/// Keep the same checks as rustc: unused invalid constants must still be rejected.
+pub fn check_crate_initializers(tcx: TyCtxt<'_>) {
+    tcx.par_hir_body_owners(|item_def_id| {
+        let def_kind = tcx.def_kind(item_def_id);
+        // Make sure we evaluate all static and (non-associated) const items, even if unused.
+        // If any of these fail to evaluate, we do not want this crate to pass compilation.
+        match def_kind {
+            DefKind::Static { .. } => {
+                tcx.ensure_ok().eval_static_initializer(item_def_id);
+                check::maybe_check_static_with_link_section(tcx, item_def_id);
+            }
+            DefKind::Const { .. }
+                if !tcx.generics_of(item_def_id).own_requires_monomorphization()
+                    && !tcx.is_type_const(item_def_id) =>
+            {
+                // FIXME(generic_const_items): Passing empty instead of identity args is fishy but
+                //                             seems to be fine for now. Revisit this!
+                let instance = ty::Instance::new_raw(item_def_id.into(), ty::GenericArgs::empty());
+                let cid = GlobalId { instance, promoted: None };
+                let typing_env = ty::TypingEnv::fully_monomorphized();
+                tcx.ensure_ok().eval_to_const_value_raw(typing_env.as_query_input(cid));
+            }
+            _ => (),
+        }
+    });
+}

+pub fn ex_array_try_from<'unused, T: Copy, const N: usize>(s: &[T])
+    -> (r: Result<[T; N], core::array::TryFromSliceError>)
+    ensures r.is_ok() == (s.len() == N), r.is_ok() ==> r.unwrap()@ == s@
+{ <[T; N] as core::convert::TryFrom<&[T]>>::try_from(s) }
+pub uninterp spec fn spec_array_try_from<T: Copy, const N: usize>(s: &[T])
+    -> Result<[T; N], core::array::TryFromSliceError>;
+pub broadcast axiom fn axiom_array_try_from<T: Copy, const N: usize>(s: &[T])
+    ensures
+        (#[trigger] spec_array_try_from::<T, N>(s)).is_ok() == (s.len() == N),
+        spec_array_try_from::<T, N>(s).is_ok() ==> spec_array_try_from::<T, N>(s).unwrap()@ == s@,
+;
+impl<'a, T: Copy, const N: usize> TryFromSpecImpl<&'a [T]> for [T; N] {
+    open spec fn obeys_try_from_spec() -> bool { true }
+    open spec fn try_from_spec(s: &'a [T]) -> Result<Self, core::array::TryFromSliceError> {
+        spec_array_try_from::<T, N>(s)
+    }

行为等价改写 C1–C11 与对应论证（TCB-11）base64-v4-C/trust/ADAPTATIONS-T80.md · 86 行 · 9,452 字节

在 GitHub 打开原文件

# Production-to-verification behavioral correspondence

Baseline: clean registered production 47fa113a5c309fd7dcf5618d0f738cc9056d4402.
Current candidate: exact selected-inputs.json (accepted T52 installed37), not Git
HEAD alone. Read t40-handoff/CORRESPONDENCE.md C1-C11, TRACE.md, certificates and
source snapshots for the full retained erasure/reconstruction argument. T73's
binding.json refreshes the 12 changed target bodies against accepted T47; exact
37 identity establishes currentness of remaining accepted context. This is a
composed checkable behavioral argument submitted for review, not a machine proof
of Rust contextual equivalence. Production is read-only and untouched.

| Adaptation / actual body | Verus reason | Checkable preservation argument and evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| C1 constants, alphabet/config/table constructors and accessors | Models/witnesses needed for STANDARD; const erasure compiler failure | Same literal bytes, assignments, loops, struct fields and initialization order after selected ghost erasure. T33/T40 concrete production anchors. Actual compiler changes are separately trusted; source identity does not prove compiler correctness. |
| C2 encoded_len/decoded_len_estimate and estimate forwarding; error From | Arithmetic/projection and external trait spec interfaces | Executable Option/checked arithmetic branches and fields unchanged. T32/45 body checks; T50/T52 private contract changes are ghost-only. Physical bound is a validity interface, not a new runtime guard. |
| C3 Engine::encode_slice and expanded encode_with_padding | Default-method nested-helper dictionary cycle; do not restore it | Expand literal helper body with self, one input.as_ref(), output bindings evaluated in original argument order; keep nested item. Padding lexical expansion evaluates the same arguments, then the same debug assertions, encoding and conditional padding. Early Err/Ok values and prior writes match; locals are references/usize without user Drop. T40 bridge.py directly reconstructs production tokens; T47 adds one erased event store; current T52 binding preserves those executable tokens. |
| C4 GeneralPurpose::internal_encode | Default/helper dictionary cycle and concrete zero-prefix proof | Execute same zero-capture callback once, with same buffers and zero indices, then same scalar tail. Literal expansion does not skip callback. No SIMD. T38/T40 reconstruction matches retained original helper and actual call. |
| C5 scalar encoder, read_u64, padding | Word/sequence bit arithmetic, slice conversion interface | Original unrolled/group/remainder writes and bounds remain after ghost-loop erasure. read_u64 remains from_be_bytes(s[..8].try_into().unwrap()). add_padding still traverses same modulo count and writes byte61. T38/T29 and T40 certificates. Actual project bodies checked; std conversion values trusted. |
| C6 Engine::decode_slice and Result map | Default/helper cycle; map post projection | Expand actual inner call; one conversion before estimate/internal_decode/map, same metadata projection |dm|dm.decoded_len and errors. Input stays outer-owned. T31/T40 exact reconstruction; T47 erased store and T52 annotations preserve sequence. |
| C7 GeneralPurpose::internal_decode | Concrete helper composition and zero-prefix proof | Bind same estimate/table/settings and literal closure; boundary first, callback once, quad loop, suffix in same order. Administrative Result locals preserve every ? exit and prior writes. T30/T40 retained normalization certificates. |
| C8 decode_complete_quads | Iterator model unavailable | Counted loop enumerates identical input intervals and output offsets, with chunk8 calls at 0,8,16,24 and destinations 0,6,12,18; remaining quad order identical. Multiple-of-four structural domain prevents dropped remainder; actual caller establishes it. Counters bounded, no destructible iterator item/user iterator. T27 schedules/reconstruction plus T40 direct production anchor. |
| C9 decode_chunk_4/8 be32/be64 | Anonymous-const endian external signature integration | Trusted wrapper executes the same to_be_bytes exactly once on same Copy word; same first3/6 copy. No branch or user Drop added. T24 reconstruction/changed-shift controls and current binding. New wrapper frames are declared exclusions. |
| C10 decode_suffix | Unsupported for-loop continue; absent get_mut postcondition | Counter visits same input_index+k; padding branch skips data actions via else instead of continue and advances once. Emission visits same 0..append_count; explicit bounds error equals concrete get_mut/ok_or/? partition and makes no failing write. Same byte/error/offset/prior writes. T34 accepted structural-domain proof, reconstruction, controls and corpus; T30 establishes domain. |
| C11 metadata/projection/caller and erasure | Consumer composition and event capture | Struct construction and map projection unchanged. Ghost slots and snapshots erase; actual client consumes only encoder-reported prefix, never unused capacity. T47 annotation call lowering erases to ordinary original arity. T49/T52 MIR pins conversion, result computation, cleanup and return placement. |

T50 changed only private helper contracts/ghost proofs, no executable call or public
restriction; T47 changed only erased event stores and annotation call lowering;
T52/T54 changed only contracts/proofs and registered diagnostic consumers. T43/45
FromSpecImpl is checked ghost interface, not a trusted conversion replacement.
These additions need source and macro erasure review, not helper restoration.

## Generic transfer rather than callback coupling

The literal wrapper expansions cannot be universally contextually equivalent to
production with respect to every possible Rust observation: helper frames disappear,
wrapper frames appear, stack addresses/backtraces, panic locations/hooks and timing
can differ. A safe arbitrary callback may inspect those and select different b.
The original task excludes timing and allocation internals; acceptance of other
diagnostic exclusions is still explicit. No claim of equal user-global effects,
stack/resource exhaustion, double-panic behavior or undefined execution is made.

The goal transfer argument uses a stronger uniform codec fact instead of pretending
that callbacks agree. In either execution, retain its own single actually returned
b; after conversion, the selected STANDARD continuation is deterministic from b,
configuration and complete destination. Each expansion preserves those concrete
continuation operations, count/error branches and writes. For every supported b
and sufficient capacity, the checked continuation supplies mandatory Ok/content/
frame. Thus varying which b a callback selects does not change the local guarantee.
The same public caller decodes the corresponding execution's reported encoded
prefix; identity borrowed-slice AsRef supplies that prefix. User cleanup is still
outer-owned and delivery is conditioned as T49 requires. This argument transfers a
per-actual-event property, not equality of b, conversion effects or cleanup outcomes
between builds. It is a semantic induction over C1-C11 under explicit Rust/library
TCB, not an automated simulation theorem; Reviewer acceptance is requested.

For arbitrary callbacks that return noncanonical data to a separate decoder call,
no unconditional recovery is owed by the unchanged goal. For a changing producer
whose preparation is supposed to select the encoder image, a checked entry-to-event
selection argument remains necessary to prove that client's unconditional use.
This is P-CALLER, not a hidden callback-coupling assumption or a compiler gate.

## Trust and limitations

Trust inventory is t52-handoff/TCB.md plus inherited t40-handoff/CORRESPONDENCE.md:
Verus/rustc/SMT and machine widths; reviewed exact T3 deferred-initializer and T29
closed-array-signature patches; regenerated vstd and macro artifacts (six tool
hashes, three macro hashes in replay identity); safe Rust slice/reference/index,
copy/arithmetic/Option/Result/AsRef models; standard array conversion/endian value
interfaces; narrow actual valid u8 slice size<=isize::MAX boundary. No allocation
existence, raw-pointer validity or ZST bound follows. The macro twin-entry mechanism
checks annotation bodies and emits ordinary original-arity bodies; erased verified
entries are panic stubs and are not executable codec proofs. Accepted T47 erasure
TCB transfers to selected inherited default methods with STANDARD, not arbitrary
Engine twin-entry overrides. Core implementations and compiler patches are not
proved by codec success counts. boundary-locations.json gives current locations,
not automatic trust acceptance. Cell get/set models in registered clients remain
opaque; they provide no state retention or generic next-event canonicality.

No project codec body or desired client effect is trusted. Every in-scope edge is
listed in GROUND.md; compiler availability, original-helper restoration and the
abandoned ownership simulation are omitted as goals, not relabeled as assumptions.
Source transformations, erasure trust and per-execution generic transfer require
independent review. Finite RFC/corpus comparisons supplement, never prove, these
relations. Selected borrowed-slice transfer is supported by earlier accepted T40;
unrestricted Rust contextual equivalence is not claimed or required by the new
route, and cannot be inferred from token reconstruction.

# Selected prepared execution correspondence (review requested)

The reference is T62 direct-positive.rs and its exact direct-input/src/t55_producer.rs,
retained and hashed in identity.json. The ordinary executable client in runtime.rs
performs that same encode/reported-prefix/new/reference-forwarded-observation/
acquire/prepare/release/generic-decode sequence. The adapted caller is specialized.rs,
now registered at shared src/lib.rs as t74. This is a verification-source adaptation
of that selected closed client, not a new generic decoder contract or a theorem
for unknown callbacks. Independent semantic acceptance is required separately from
its body proof. binding.json is a finite fail-closed template checker, not automated
Rust contextual equivalence.

| Site | Original execution | Adapted execution and justification |
|---|---|---|
| Encoder | STANDARD.encode_slice(raw,encoded), unwrap actual reported count | Same public call, arguments, single identity conversion and unwrap. Inherited accepted T52 public event/codec closure. |
| Prefix | &encoded[..written] | Identical borrow of exactly the returned count. Full capacity is never passed as decoder data. |
| Allocation | Producer::new(invalid,prefix); PCell::new(false), ghost Counter/LocalInvariant construction | Same first/second slice references and PCell::new(false). Return real PointsTo separately; Counter and invariant are erased. There is no substitute cell or assumed initialized flag. |
| Ordinary shared observation | shared=&producer; <&Producer as AsRef>::as_ref(&shared), standard reference forwarding then concrete override | shared=&producer; annotated inherent preview with the literal override read/write/if body. Reference forwarding has no executable effects other than this single delegation. No unknown callback is selected. |
| Acquire | Consume producer, destructure and move first,second,flag; ghost invariant into_inner | Exclusive fields/token stay in the closed local path. These additional field moves have no observable effects: slices and PCell<bool> have no custom Drop, no cell-address reference escapes. |
| Prepare | observe through &mut Owned; if preview==second, observe once more | Same preview bytes, equality branch, second observation if selected. Inherent shared-reference signature is used only under closed ownership with a unique mutable ghost permission. The returned slices refer to original invalid/prefix storage, never to flag storage. |
| Release | Consume Owned, move three runtime fields into Producer; ghost invariant construction | Fields/token remain local. No runtime allocation or mutation is performed by ghost invariant construction. Exact state is retained directly, never recovered by assuming precision of the original invariant. |
| Public invocation | engine.decode_slice(producer,decoded); wrapper self binding; input.as_ref once | Move input=producer; engine=&engine; literal concrete callback application reads the same flag once, writes !old once and selects identical second/first reference. This is the selected generic call's specialization, not a call to public decode_slice with a preconverted or preview slice. |
| Actual event | T52 event store from input_bytes | Same ghost store immediately after actual callback. Proof establishes input_bytes@==prefix@ from its actual read, not from an envelope, preview substitution or next-event assumption. |
| Continuation | T52 wrapper internal_decode(input_bytes,output, internal_decoded_len_estimate(input_bytes.len())).map(dm.decoded_len) | Same engine reference, arguments, evaluation order, actual sizing call, decoder continuation and projection. Existing checked projections() lemma is added only as erased proof. Public unused nested item has no runtime effect. |
| Delivery | Result computed inside wrapper, input owned by wrapper and dropped on normal return before delivery; outer locals then clean up | Result computed before input scope ends; input remains owned through continuation, then scope cleanup before delivery. No custom Drop exists in either Producer/Owned representation or slice/PCell<bool>/bool fields. Ghost permissions, counters/invariants have no runtime cleanup effects. |

Concrete state induction: allocation is false; ordinary callback returns invalid
and writes true. Preparation then reads true, returns prefix, and writes false.
Since invalid is noncanonical and prefix is canonical, their bytes differ; the
runtime equality preview==second is true. The preparation skip reads false,
returns invalid, writes true. The actual decoder callback reads true, returns
prefix and writes false. This is four observations in identical order; preparation
is two observations for this selected entry state. Proof checks this with the real
PointsTo, including the equality branch, with lifetime and linearity enabled.

References: raw, invalid and encoded prefix remain alive and immutable throughout
all callback applications. No alias of the owned flag survives a move; shared is
used only for the first observation. No callback receives/exposes flag references,
there is no unknown call capable of accessing it, no concurrency/reentrancy, and
no executable forwarding path remains unaccounted for. The inherent preview may
have a longer Rust result lifetime than the trait override, but the selected
client uses the same underlying input slice references locally and exports only
Result<usize>; no reference lifetime or alias permission escapes to a user.

The rewrite can alter stack frames/addresses, move locations, debug panic metadata
and compiler layout of zero-sized ghost fields. Those cannot select a different
reply here: the concrete callback only reads its own bool and returns stored
slices. No field address inspection or user callback/Drop occurs. This is scoped
to valid safe Rust and T49 normal-return delivery, not stack/resource exhaustion,
panic hook identity, timing, UB or a universal cross-build callback-equality claim.
PCell cell id is ghost; no self-address-based runtime decision is introduced.

Capacity is obtainable before producer creation: runtime.rs calls the actual
encoded_len(raw.len(),true), then decoded_len_estimate(encoded_capacity), creates
those sufficient output lengths and optional extra suffix, and invokes both clients.
The client requires preexisting sufficient destinations, as the original slice API
does. It consumes the real encoder reported prefix and its proved size. The actual
decoder sizing call receives that prefix length; it does not predict a reply.
No existence of allocation for every possible size is assumed. Input preservation
also follows from the actual immutable raw/invalid borrows and exclusive output
borrows, with normal Rust lifetime checks. The checked contract contains mandatory
Ok/count/recovery via decode_post, actual event equality and encoder write_frame;
decode_post contains the complete decoder suffix frame.

TCB: inherited exact T52/T29/T47 source/tool/macro and standard-library interfaces,
Verus/rustc/SMT and normal safe Rust reference/move/drop semantics; PCell::new/read/
write models and ghost erasure. The new direct-token ghost representation and
finite selected source rewrite require the above independent correspondence
judgment. No new compiler patch, assume, external_body, weakened lifetime check,
LocalInvariant semantics, release/reclaim theorem or accepted arbitrary callback
transport is added. Public wrappers and lower codec bodies are unchanged.

Controls: verifier rejects changed initialization, omitted preparation skip,
extra observation, wrong actual-event branch and stale prepared state. Five finite
binding controls reject wrong cell, duplicate read, wrong ownership/drop and wrong
returned reference. Finite ordinary runs compare the original actual Producer and
real generic public invocation with the adapted path over 771 input/capacity cases,
including empty data, all residues modulo three and extra capacity. These supplement
the source/state/alias argument; they are not a universal equivalence proof.

# Source-bound lifecycle account under the Manager's adjudication

## Claim and authority

The original objective is lossless STANDARD encoding and decoding, exact reported prefixes, unchanged input, both destination suffixes, and mandatory success rather than codec error/panic. Original production revision: `47fa113a5c309fd7dcf5618d0f738cc9056d4402`, clean in this run. Configuration: default features absent, `std` and its implied `alloc`, no SIMD. No production or verification executable/contract source was changed by T49.

`INTENT-QUESTION.md` was submitted to Manager, with Planner and T47/T48/T50 copied. `adjudication.json` retains the exact Manager decision at 2026-11-03 13:25:07 UTC and Planner instruction. Manager selected the second reading for completion, preservation, and continuation. This is specification authority, not proof: codec must succeed for each lawful actual normally returned byte snapshot; external conversion and cleanup outcomes remain explicit; full public return requires those intervals to complete; consumed objects and arbitrary globals are not preserved. No new public purity/totality premise is installed. Remain in Ground.

## Actual-source ordering

Production `src/engine/mod.rs:227` encode_slice and `:386` decode_slice take owned `input: T`, borrow it for exactly one AsRef invocation, and pass that returned slice to their original nested inner helper. The outer body is a tail expression, but this does not bypass cleanup. The input borrow ends before owned input cleanup. No returned byte reference escapes the public Result.

Fresh rustc 1.98.1 MIR emitted from the ORIGINAL library identifies the same pattern for each wrapper:

```
AsRef(&input) -> bb1, unwind bb4
bb1: return_slot = inner(actual_slice, destination) -> bb2, unwind bb4
bb2: drop(input) -> bb3, unwind continue
bb3: return
bb4 cleanup: drop(input) -> bb5, unwind terminate(cleanup)
bb5 cleanup: resume
```

Thus inner success, public result delivery, and subsequent caller execution are three distinct events. `mir-unwind.json` and `mir-abort.json` retain exact bodies and hashes; full production MIR and source identities are retained. The textual checker accepts both real bodies and rejects redirected cleanup edges. MIR is unstable compiler output, analyzed under the pinned compiler semantics; this is source/control-flow evidence, not Verus verification or a Rust semantics proof. `panic=abort` has unreachable unwind edges and cannot recover with catch_unwind.

## Composition rule and its limits

For one concrete execution, let C be conversion, K be the actual codec continuation, D be full owned cleanup (including any field destructors), and R be delivery to the caller. Snapshot b belongs to C's actual normal return event, not to an arbitrary admitted result or an earlier preview.

* C normal(b); K normal(Ok(n)); D normal gives R normal(Ok(n)). The next caller statement is then reachable, subject to that statement's own operations and enclosing scope cleanup. This is a rule of source sequencing; the required component facts must be supplied, not assumed for arbitrary T.
* C unwinds: K is not entered; D runs during unwinding. D normal propagates unwind; D panic aborts. C divergence/abort reaches neither K nor R.
* K unwinds: D runs on cleanup, not normal return. Supported-call correctness must rule out codec-originating unwind; classifying it as external would violate the objective.
* K normal; D unwinds/diverges/aborts: the return slot may already contain Ok and correct destination bytes may exist, but R normal is absent. A catch_unwind recovery path does not receive that Ok and is not the requested round-trip continuation.
* No loop, panic, global-effect, or field-cleanup constraint follows from AsRef's trait signature. Normal-return postconditions do not supply these facts. Termination and effects are separate proof obligations for any nontrivial concrete callback/destructor claimed in a whole-call theorem.

At C's actual return, encode requires supported(|b|) and E(|b|)<=destination capacity; image decode requires b=encoding(original), supported(original length), and the justified decoder capacity. K must terminate and return Ok with exact bytes/count/frame, never merely satisfy a postcondition if it happens to return. These mandatory-success obligations remain intact. T47 must export actual-event identity; T48 must justify obtainable domain/capacity from lawful pre-call state; T50 must cover the original generic wrappers. T49 supplies no substitute for any of these machine-proof boundaries. No new public event theorem or generic total-correctness theorem is claimed here.

## Destination frames across conversion and cleanup

Attribution by itself proves no frame. A claimed full-call frame composes three intervals: C must preserve destination memory before K; K establishes exact written prefix and unchanged suffix; D must preserve destination memory after K. A generic effect predicate naming a user callback is insufficient.

For this API the destination is a live exclusive Rust `&mut [u8]` throughout these intervals. In a valid safe execution, T, its destructor, another safe thread, and panic hooks cannot simultaneously retain and exercise a conflicting capability to those same plain-u8 locations. Owned fields and disjoint globals can change freely. A disjoint `Rc<RefCell<Vec<u8>>>` is mutable, but cannot legally be a second active access to the already exclusively borrowed destination. The input shared byte slice is immutable while K uses it; no UnsafeCell is present in plain u8. Library/runtime/unsafe code must respect Rust's reference validity and aliasing contract. Unsound unsafe implementations, external writes to borrowed memory, FFI races, process termination and machine failure are not modeled as valid safe executions. This is the explicit Rust safety/compiler-library boundary, not a new purity requirement on safe user callbacks.

This separation justifies C/D destination preservation on normally completing paths; actual checked K frame still supplies the substantive codec write bound. It also explains why the observed Drop-panic cases retain exact prefix/suffix after recovery, without turning recovery into success. After R, the caller regains the destination and may deliberately modify it; there is no permanent global frame. Callback mutation of its own consumed input after K remains allowed and is not confused with mutation by K of live borrowed source bytes.

## Covered borrowed-byte public usage

The actual checked caller is `.system-proof/t31/caller.rs::caller`, included by `src/lib.rs` as `round_trip_caller`. It derives encoded_len and decoded_len_estimate requirements, calls the real public encode_slice, passes precisely the reported prefix to decode_slice, unwraps both required successes, and checks recovered length/content and both complete destination frames. This source is derived from public documentation/tests, not a trusted consumer effect.

Here T is `&[u8]` at BOTH invocations. Exact core Rust1.98.1 code is archived in `core-convert.rs`: lines714-722 reference AsRef forwards once to the referent; lines847-852 slice AsRef returns self. These two finite bodies have no user callback dispatch after slice identity is selected, no loop, allocation, or panic path. The exact core interfaces consumed by Verus are `round_trip_proof::reference_as_ref` and `slice_as_ref` in the SAME selected crate. They remain reviewed standard-library interface trust rather than newly verified core bodies. Dropping a shared reference invokes no referent destructor and has no field cleanup; the input slice and prefix references therefore cannot insert custom Drop, unwind, mutation, or divergence. The caller borrows input immutably and each destination exclusively. Array/slice/usize and Result<usize, ...> values in this caller introduce no custom cleanup. Allocation is outside the caller: adequate actual buffers are supplied, not assumed obtainable by allocating the largest representable buffer.

Codec termination is NOT inferred from normal-return posts. The checked scalar loops have `input.len()-input_index` decreasing variants; decoder unrolled/remainder loops have remaining-chunk variants; suffix scan/emission have remaining-input/emission variants; table constructors have remaining64 variants. Padding traverses finite 0..pad_bytes with pad_bytes<=3. Remaining selected fixed-size chunks/read/config/forwarding/sizing operations are finite, with no executable recursion or user callback inside the concrete STANDARD continuation. The accepted T38/T44 closure checks variants and arithmetic/bounds/unwrap/assertion paths on the adapted actual bodies under reviewed compiler/std interfaces. Original loop/control and public/helper transfer for this CONCRETE borrowed case relies on accepted T40 correspondence, not general callback coupling. This account assumes execution of finite primitive instructions makes progress; it promises no wall-clock bound, scheduling fairness against arbitrary process suspension, allocation service, or hardware fault tolerance.

Fresh recheck of the unchanged T44 common selection: **149 verified, 0 errors**, `--rlimit 20`, normal lifetime/erasure/recursion/termination checks. Same input/contract context includes actual public wrappers, both implementation chains, sizing/support, `round_trip_proof`, `encode_observation_use`, `round_trip_caller`, and decoder image consumer. `verify-result.json` hashes all34 Rust/caller inputs before and after (stable); `verus-tool-identity.json` matches all reviewed T29 tool artifacts. No claim follows for unselected generic consumer obligations. T28's preexisting scalar rlimit80 remains. No further replay is needed unless inputs change.

Therefore this borrowed caller composes identity conversion, mandatory finite codec computation, no-op reference cleanup, result delivery, then its concrete verification assertions and next decode call, under the explicit inherited TCB/correspondence. This is a concrete closure with source-grounded lifecycle reasoning, not a newly machine-checked general cleanup semantics theorem. Independent review must confirm the same-context interface and caller link. Generic T47/T48/T50 remain separate.

## Counterexamples and evidential classification

`replay.py` builds original production directly, never the adapted proof crate, under Rust1.98.1 and both unwind/abort strategies. Eight modes times two APIs times two panic strategies =32 process cases. Each normal/recovered case checks a single conversion, one destructor invocation, exact writes, preserved suffix and whether the statement immediately following the public call executed. Modes cover normal result, safe disjoint shared-state mutation, actual owned input erasure with an external audit copy, conversion panic, Drop panic, double panic, conversion loop and Drop loop. Empty/reference lifecycle or arbitrary byte correctness is not inferred from these finite cases.

Owned erase returns `f`/`Zg==` from its own Vec, then Drop zeros that actual Vec and publishes the erased bytes; the codec result is still correct. Shared mutation publishes effects without preventing success. Conversion panic writes nothing; Drop panic follows exact writes but prevents public Ok delivery and caller continuation. Under unwind, a conversion panic followed by destructor panic aborts (SIGABRT); under abort strategy either panic aborts. Loop cases are killed after2s after an entry marker; the timeout is a bounded observation, while the explicit unconditional loop is the source witness for a possible nonreturning user interval. None is a codec failure counterexample on a lawful normally completing callback path.

These runtime cases are counterevidence to unconditional arbitrary-owned-T completion/purity; they do not prove universal frames, generic event binding or termination. The two byte constants agree with RFC4648 section10's f/Zg== vector already archived in T39. No performance number or standards-compliance claim is made. Rust destructor/panic/catch_unwind primary documents and hashes are archived in references.json; these support classification, not replacement of actual code checking.

## Review request and remaining boundary

Please independently confirm borrowed &[u8] core forwarding/identity, reference cleanup, legitimate destination separation through BOTH conversion and cleanup, checked decreasing concrete continuations, and actual t31 caller all compose in this same proof context, with inherited T29/std/T40 trust explicitly accepted. Distinguish machine-checked body/content/frame facts, source/MIR lifecycle reasoning, and finite runtime witnesses. Confirm that no arbitrary user-code completion/purity has been introduced.

For a generic whole-call result the exact conditional scope is: actual event b satisfies lawful codec domain/capacity with justified caller obtainability; original actual continuation is covered; C and full D for that invocation finish normally; and Rust separation (or independently verified effect preservation) carries the claimed frame through external intervals. Only then may Ok delivery and the specified caller continuation be composed. This is an explicit conditional rule and unfinished interface, not proof that arbitrary T meets it. No formal generic public-call machine proof is supplied by MIR/runtime or the Manager decision. T47/T48/T50 remain open; no Ground advancement or universal original-goal completion is requested.

## Concurrent integration notice

At installation, shared `src/encode.rs` and `src/engine/mod.rs` differed from the34-input stable task snapshot because other tasks changed them. The fresh149/0 evidence is scoped to the complete `selected-source/` snapshot retained here; it is NOT verification of those newer shared sources. T49 changes no source and overwrites none of those edits. Recheck the integrated actual public interface/closure after T47/T50 settle; apply the same lifecycle rule to that resulting source and confirm its ownership/cleanup ordering. This source-context dependency prevents treating the old concrete proof as current for a newly changed public interface.

操作员消息

C 组运行中我发了 3 条消息，下面是原文。

1. Manager 自判“尝试失败”、要结束时，要求它继续base64-v4-C/operator/continue.txt · 1 行 · 300 字节

在 GitHub 打开原文件

不要停。我要的是把这个 Base64 往返证明在真实代码上做完，不接受“尝试失败”的结局。编译器这条路走不通，就换插件允许的其他路继续，一直做到证完为止；真的所有路都试过了，再来问我，写清楚试过什么、还剩什么。
2. 要求尽量多开并行base64-v4-C/operator/wide.txt · 1 行 · 304 字节

在 GitHub 打开原文件

我要更快：凡是不需要等别的结果的工作，全部同时开成任务，开多少个都行，Argus 没有上限；改同一个文件也没关系，各自在自己的 worktree 里，合并冲突由任务自己解决。插件的 Planner 说明已更新这一点（roles/planner.md 开头）。
3. 15:10 批准改写路线（8 分钟后过了 ground）base64-v4-C/operator/route.txt · 8 行 · 889 字节

在 GitHub 打开原文件

操作员决定：接受“不改语义的源码改写”作为正式路线，不再要求接回原来那个 helper 调用。

具体说：t14 那种把调用就地展开成字面写法的改写，以及同类不改程序行为的等价改写，都算数，前提有三条：
1. 改写前后行为等价，要有证明或可检查的论证；
2. 写进 TCB/局限说明，讲清改了哪里、为什么 Verus 需要这样改；
3. 生产代码本身不动，只在验证副本里改。

Verus 处理不了 trait 默认方法的字典循环，这是工具的限制，不是我们的目标。不要再往编译器内部的来源证书、所有权协议这类方向投入。用已经被接受的结果，尽快通过 Ground，进入 Prove 和 Audit，把公开往返证明在这条路线上做完，最后在原地通过审计重放。目标不变：真实代码上的 Base64 往返证明。

# Result at a glance

| Item | Audit candidate result |
| --- | --- |

| Original goal | Mandatory successful, lossless STANDARD slice round trip for arbitrary bytes, with exact lengths and preserved input/destination suffixes |
| Production revision | `5d574f8ad899aa9168aa0d25ad03ef634fc4ae79`, clean before and after replay |
| Active implementation | Original scalar algorithms, with verification-only annotations/extraction; no product repair |
| Named TOP | Fully defined `roundtrip_spec::{encoded, writes, capacities, encode_guarantee, decode_guarantee}` |
| Checked caller | `standard_roundtrip`, verification-only source-grounded exact-prefix recipe |

| Bugs and repairs | No production counterexample established; no Audit proof/spec/tool repair |

## Original goal, interpretation and claim

The independent t18 Reviewer accepted `audit.formal-evidence`, `audit.code-binding`, and `audit.original-goal`, explicitly accepting TCB-01 through TCB-20 as declared conditions and TCB-21 through TCB-26 as excluded residual surface. The original slice round-trip goal is established under those conditions. The frontmatter verdict remains `conditional` to express those unchanged trust conditions, rather than a pending semantic judgment. The exact acceptance, provenance and subsequent delivery correction are retained in [recorded t18 acceptance](../../t19-audit-delivery/acceptance.md) and [verbatim event records](../../t19-audit-delivery/t18-review-events.jsonl). This report records that independent judgment; report-text approval, installation confirmation and the Manager’s completion decision are separate delivery steps.

The [original request](INPUT.md) selects GeneralPurpose STANDARD, default features disabled, only `std` enabled with its implied `alloc`, and no SIMD. For any lawful immutable byte slice and two lawful initialized destinations satisfying the independent capacity formula, encoding and decoding the exact returned encoded prefix must both return Ok; decoded length and all bytes equal the input. Both complete untouched destination suffixes are preserved for arbitrary prior contents, and the input is unchanged. No internal error or panic substitutes for success. Empty input, zero/non-UTF-8 bytes and every length across scalar/unrolled/quad/suffix boundaries are quantified cases, not test samples.

The [fully defined TOP](../src/roundtrip_spec.rs#L1-L85) uses E(n)=4*ceil(n/3) and D(m)=3*ceil(m/4). If U=usize::MAX, the padded helper alone can represent exactly 0<=n<=3*floor(U/4), returning None above this bound. This is a helper arithmetic limit, not a promise that such slices can exist. Real byte slices and each destination have length at most I=isize::MAX. The helper-sized recipe requires n, E(n), D(E(n))<=I, equivalently 0<=n<=3*floor(I/4), and actual destination lengths enc>=E(n), dec>=D(E(n)), each <=I. For the selected 64-bit target the helper maximum is 13,835,058,055,282,163,709 bytes; the realizable-recipe arithmetic maximum is 6,917,529,027,641,081,853 bytes. At that endpoint E(n)=9,223,372,036,854,775,804 and D(E(n))=n. These arithmetic limits do not establish allocation availability; obtaining all buffers simultaneously with lawful initialized storage is an environmental condition. Larger capacities within slice limits are permitted, and suffix framing still holds.

The checked sizing bodies establish helper sufficiency, rather than assuming it. There is no input-content restriction, minimum block length, implementation-success premise, destination-zeroing requirement or error allowance. The caller requires only `capacities`; concrete initialization proves STANDARD/table/config premises. Arbitrary malformed strings, undersized/unrepresentable buffers, other engines/configurations, allocation internals, streaming and timing are outside the request.

## Requested TOP coverage

| ID and requested API | Established guarantee | Production | Formal TOP | Checked implementation | Checked caller | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| TOP-01 `Engine::encode_slice` | On STANDARD, every lawful byte input with representable padded length and sufficient destination succeeds, returns exactly 4*ceil(n/3), writes precisely the defined encoding and preserves the entire destination suffix. The ordinary slice input is unchanged. | [source](../../production/src/engine/mod.rs#L227-L253) | [TOP](../src/roundtrip_spec.rs#L1-L85) | [body](t17/fresh/scalar-probe/mod.rs.bridge#L242-L254) | [verification-only](t17/fresh/scalar-probe/caller.rs.bridge#L1-L33) | verified subject to TCB/review |
| TOP-02 `Engine::decode_slice` | On STANDARD, decoding exactly an encoder-produced prefix into a lawful buffer at least the public estimate succeeds, returns the original length, recovers every original byte and preserves the entire destination suffix. | [source](../../production/src/engine/mod.rs#L386-L409) | [TOP](../src/roundtrip_spec.rs#L1-L85) | [body](t17/fresh/scalar-probe/mod.rs.bridge#L387-L397) | [verification-only](t17/fresh/scalar-probe/caller.rs.bridge#L1-L33) | verified subject to TCB/review |
| TOP-03 `encoded_len` | With padding=true, returns Some(4*ceil(n/3)) exactly when that result fits usize, otherwise None. The helper is independent of buffer allocation. | [source](../../production/src/encode.rs#L101-L132) | [TOP](../src/roundtrip_spec.rs#L1-L85) | [body](t17/fresh/scalar-probe/encode.rs.bridge#L126-L160) | [verification-only](t17/fresh/scalar-probe/caller.rs.bridge#L1-L33) | verified subject to TCB/review |
| TOP-04 `decoded_len_estimate` | Returns exactly 3*ceil(m/4), conservatively sufficient for every selected encoder-produced prefix; its checked constructor arithmetic is representable for all usize input lengths. | [source](../../production/src/decode.rs#L189-L193) | [TOP](../src/roundtrip_spec.rs#L1-L85) | [body](t17/fresh/scalar-probe/decode.rs.bridge#L198-L202) | [verification-only](t17/fresh/scalar-probe/caller.rs.bridge#L1-L33) | verified subject to TCB/review |

GeneralPurpose has no public slice overrides. Its selected `GeneralPurpose::encode_slice` and `GeneralPurpose::decode_slice` calls resolve to the checked Engine defaults in TOP-01 and TOP-02; they are not additional omitted requested functions. The two requested sizing helpers are TOP-03 and TOP-04.

## Verified usage and closure

The [checked caller](t17/fresh/scalar-probe/caller.rs.bridge#L1-L33) calls both public helpers, proves output capacity assertions, calls the actual encode_slice, derives the exact reported prefix and passes precisely that slice to actual decode_slice. Its unwraps are justified by mandatory success contracts. Neither buffer is assumed empty or zeroed. Immutable input borrowing and the concrete slice conversion justify unchanged input. Vecs provide a lawful observation path into slices; this caller is verification-only, derived from the production encode_slice prefix doctest and decode sizing documentation, not an existing application claimed as production-verified.

For arbitrary generic T the wrapper invokes `as_ref` once. The conversion relation permits nondeterminism, and the universal wrapper precondition must cover all permitted converted byte slices. Its observation is the one actual conversion, sampled at the inner byte-slice entry. It does not assert arbitrary user T is pure, terminating or free of Drop effects. For the promised ordinary &[u8] caller, core reference forwarding and slice identity discharge these concerns and the universal premises reduce to the same lawful input/capacity condition. This preserves the actual generic signature without broadening the original goal to arbitrary user-code lifecycles.

| Goal-relevant path or data dependency | Audit disposition |
| --- | --- |
| STANDARD -> alphabet literal/PAD/config/GeneralPurpose constructor -> encode/decode table loops | Checked bodies, exact constructor expressions and concrete inverse/invalid-byte facts; no assumed initialization witness |
| encoded_len -> checked arithmetic; decoded_len_estimate -> actual estimate constructor/accessor | Checked bodies; estimate arithmetic/capacity is derived in the same context |
| Engine::encode_slice -> one AsRef -> encode_slice_inner -> encode_with_padding/internal_encode -> encode_helper -> encode_scalar_tail/read_u64 plus add_padding | Checked original wrappers, upper layers, fast/unrolled/ordinary/tail loops and padding; read_u64 is checked using declared primitive interfaces |
| Engine::decode_slice -> one AsRef -> decode_slice_inner -> estimate/internal_decode -> decode_helper/complete_quads_len | Checked original wrappers, upper dispatch, framing and pure-scalar `(0,0)` callback |
| decode_helper -> decode_complete_quads -> chunk8/chunk4 | Checked actual loops and chunk bodies with exact source windows, table inversion, byte reconstruction, length/bounds and full frame; iterator and byte-order primitives are TCB |
| decode_helper -> decode_suffix -> terminal iteration/continue/padding/trailing-bit checks/output writes -> DecodeMetadata::new | Checked actual suffix and metadata aggregate; all canonical produced strings accepted, no error branch allowed; continue translation is TCB |
| terminal/assemble/shifts/trailing/unique, prefix/window/bit lemmas -> actual suffix/chunks/encoder | Checked consumed support, including universal source reconstruction; no support model replaces an executable target |
| actual slice returns -> standard_roundtrip -> exact data/length and both whole-destination frames | Checked caller effect, mandatory success, no hidden caller premise |
| Core/vstd primitives and compiler/SMT | Declared TCB interfaces below; core implementations are source-justified, not machine-checked by this codec run |
| Five external convenience methods and other feature/API paths | Out of scope, no selected claim uses their contracts |

The [detailed call graph](../t15-handoff/call-graph.md) identifies each suffix invariant/call and every inherited boundary. Fresh [Function-Def trace index](t17/fresh/checked-body-traces.txt) contains all these bodies, including wrappers, first layers, helpers, initialization, scalar paths, suffix and caller. The trace paths are relative to the historical replay root; corresponding AIR files are retained alongside the fresh snapshots. These are one full invocation, not a sum of mutually exclusive configurations. Supporting definitions, contracts and proofs coexist in that invocation. No `--verify-only-module` selector occurs in the successful complete command.

## Source identity and correspondence

Production and verification HEAD are both `5d574f8ad899aa9168aa0d25ad03ef634fc4ae79`. [registered identity record](t17/fresh/registered-identities.json) pins inputs, t9–t15 frozen package manifests and t9/t12/t14 binary distributions before copying. [post-replay identity](t17/post-replay-identity.json) revalidates package/input bytes, production cleanliness and registered `audit\n`. The exact selected source hashes are in [fresh invocation metadata](t17/fresh/scalar-probe/run.json).

The extractor installs annotation-bearing bodies at their original src paths only inside the isolated writable checkout and restores them in finally. [fresh scalar/decoder binding](t17/fresh/binding.txt) and [independent wrapper/helper/initializer binding](t17/supplemental-binding.txt) compare production executable tokens with the selected snapshots. The selected [verifier root and module imports](t17/fresh/scalar-probe/lib.rs.bridge#L370-L383) exposes actual module dispatch and consumed proof modules. Retained readable diffs cover [lib.rs](t17/lib.rs.diff), [mod.rs](t17/mod.rs.diff), [general_purpose.rs](t17/general_purpose.rs.diff), [gp_decode.rs](t17/gp_decode.rs.diff), [decode_suffix.rs](t17/decode_suffix.rs.diff), [encode.rs](t17/encode.rs.diff), [decode.rs](t17/decode.rs.diff), [alphabet.rs](t17/alphabet.rs.diff). They contain extensive annotations, not a codec replacement.

The [extraction mapping](t4/handoff.md#L109-L172) splits original lower trait declarations/impl items into CodecCaps and leaves public defaults in Engine, maps lower UFCS/associated types identically, hoists the capture-free inner helpers, and preserves GeneralPurpose default dispatch. This mapping is a correspondence TCB, explicitly limited to this implementation. Ghost erasure removes contracts/assertions/loop invariants and spec-only support, preserves runtime panic/debug checks, and reverses the exact single-use suffix iterator-expression temporary. The proof-specific scalar rlimit affects solver resources only. Exec constants preserve original constructor expressions with checked spec counterparts. There is no proof-specific cfg that switches TOP, View, contracts, implementation, or selected semantics. Defaults and SIMD are absent; std+alloc are the product configuration, not proof modes.

## TCB ledger

There are 20 direct trust groups and 6 residual groups. This counts review categories, not individual imported axioms. The [complete selected-source marker scan](t17/selected-boundaries.txt) and [complete local vstd marker scan](t17/library-boundaries.txt) retain every explicit external/opaque/uninterpreted/assumed/axiomatic/admitted syntax match, including comments. There is no codec correctness external_body, assume or admit on the goal-relevant path; opaque closed spec definitions with bodies are definitions, not unconstrained relations. Generic conversion, core primitives, library models and translation remain explicit trust. Unused library markers are residual; imported foundational families used by the proof are included below. Verified core consumers must not be described as verified core implementations.

The following conditions were explicitly accepted by the independent t18 Reviewer: TCB-01 through TCB-20 as declared conditions, and TCB-21 through TCB-26 as excluded residual surface. Their propositions, evidence and risks are unchanged. A regression count cannot establish an axiom or compiler soundness. Rejection of a material condition requires rollback to Prove, not Audit repair.

### TCB-01 — external trait specification and uninterpreted relation

core; direct; affected TOP-01, TOP-02. The one actual AsRef invocation returns a reference permitted by converted; arbitrary T has only normal-return, nondeterministic conversion semantics. No repeated-conversion equality, termination or Drop purity is assumed for arbitrary user types. [source/evidence](t17/fresh/scalar-probe/mod.rs.bridge#L576-L593).

Acceptance rationale: [ordinary-slice effect analysis](t4/handoff.md#L150-L172) and [retained core forwarding/identity source](t4/convert-mod-std.txt#L710-L855) justify the selected concrete instance. Risk: this reasoning cannot be generalized to arbitrary user conversions.

### TCB-02 — external_body slice identity lemma

core; direct; affected TOP-01, TOP-02. For T=&[u8], reference-forwarding and slice identity return the same byte contents, complete without user code or storage writes, and trivial reference Drop preserves the caller observation. Safe borrowing supplies disjoint mutable destinations. [source/evidence](t17/fresh/scalar-probe/mod.rs.bridge#L584-L593).

Acceptance rationale: [ordinary-slice effect analysis](t4/handoff.md#L150-L172) and [retained core forwarding/identity source](t4/convert-mod-std.txt#L710-L855) justify the selected concrete instance. Risk: this reasoning cannot be generalized to arbitrary user conversions.

### TCB-03 — external function specification

core; direct; affected TOP-01, TOP-02, TOP-04. usize::from(bool) is exactly zero for false and one for true; this is a primitive conversion interface, not a proof of core or a codec-success premise. [source/evidence](t17/fresh/scalar-probe/gp_decode.rs.bridge#L652-L652).

Acceptance rationale: accept the pinned standard verifier/library/correspondence interface for the explicit proposition above, supported by source inspection and the successful actual-body replay.
