## Verification and Audit evidence

From the workspace root, the durable audit launcher is:

```bash
python3 verification/.system-proof/t17/replay-audit.py
```

It requires a new `t17-audit-replay` sibling destination. Its [source](t17/replay-audit.py) runs the frozen t15 replay in memory with only two operational adaptations: it checks the registered phase is Audit, and labels the isolated copy Prove for historical replay/binding assertions. No frozen package is edited, and no registered phase is written. [launcher identity](t17/launcher-identity.json) and [outer readable output](t17/replay.log) make this explicit. The historical printed “remain in Prove” refers only to the copy and carries no campaign authority. For a new review run, pass `--destination /new/writable/path`; the unchanged source/package/tool hash validation remains mandatory.

The launcher invokes these serial commands in the copy:

```bash
python3 t15-handoff/regress.py
python3 t15-handoff/probe.py
python3 t15-handoff/check-binding.py --production /fastdata/v-boxiuli/noah/exp/ts/production
```

Exact commands, timestamps, expected markers and readable output are retained in [fresh driver records](t17/fresh/replay-results.json). The complete proof command is:

```text
<t14 distribution>/verus src/lib.rs --cap-lints allow --crate-type lib --edition 2021
  --cfg 'feature="std"' --cfg 'feature="alloc"' --trace --log vir --log air
  --log-dir t15-handoff/scalar-probe/ir
```

The executable is Verus `0.2026.09.29.d85f757.t14`, release linux_x86_64, Rust `1.98.1-x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu`. The original Verus source is d85f75780de687461a30e7771985c4a3e9ea0e58, with reviewed t9 normalization/conversion, t12 iterator/library and t14 macro/backend continue patches. [tool source manifest](../t14-handoff/tool-source-sha256.json) and [binary/library/solver manifest](../t14-handoff/adapted-binary-sha256.json) identify actual adapted inputs; synthetic build Git metadata is not the source identity. The fresh invocation records distribution hashes, and the pre-copy driver checks all retained distributions. No installed tools were edited or rebuilt during Audit. Fresh [tool correspondence checks](t17/tool-identity.json) verify all 1464 original and adapted source-manifest entries, exactly the six reviewed changed files, installed binary/library hashes, adapted distribution hashes and clean original installed source. The build-to-binary relationship still relies explicitly on retained reviewed build evidence, not a version string.

Fresh scope is the full extracted target crate, 146 obligations, 0 errors, exit 0, including source-grounded caller and every selected codec body. [readable verifier output](t17/fresh/scalar-probe/verify.log) and [VIR root](t17/fresh/scalar-probe/ir/crate.vir) expose input/body correspondence; the [AIR query index](t17/fresh/checked-body-traces.txt) locates actual Function-Def queries. ## Findings, bugs and repairs

No production data-loss counterexample or required product repair was established. The active claim concerns the original registered algorithms. Prior codec proof gaps are closed by checked bodies; the former core-interface and continue-lowering limitations are handled by explicitly trusted, source-supported local adaptations rather than by trusting codec correctness. Audit introduced only evidence/report scripts and artifacts. One evidence collector initially assumed every historical task had inputs.json; the collector now handles absent optional manifests as the frozen replay already does. This had no effect on proof execution or candidate meaning.

Historical specdet underconstraint analysis was inconclusive owing to quantifiers, not an accepted completeness proof. Its original artifacts remain linked in the t4 handoff. The defined byte relation, source-grounded caller, mandatory-success/frame guarantees and checked whole-output uniqueness provide reviewable semantic evidence; a specialist tool outcome does not replace original-goal judgment.

## Remaining limitations and independent decision

The result depends on explicit primitive/library/compiler/correspondence/environment trust, not on assumed codec success. Core integer/array/iterator implementations are justified by retained source, not fully verified Rust core bodies. There is no allocation-existence theorem, all-platform compiler theorem, arbitrary generic-conversion lifecycle theorem, other-configuration claim, malformed-string guarantee, or proof of the five convenience APIs. These limits preserve the original byte-slice round-trip objective and its lawful capacity domain.

The independent t18 Reviewer accepted the three protected Audit items and all listed TCB groups with the classifications above, finding no rollback or substantive repair necessary. That letter reports a fresh independent replay of 146 verified, 0 errors, four passing inherited regression suites, source/support hashes matching t17, and inspection supporting correspondence. The unavailable /tmp evidence package is not substituted for durable evidence or used as an installation source. The t17 evidence links and source identities below remain unchanged. The later t18 delivery correction left approved-finalization installation unconfirmed. This t19 report records semantic acceptance; completion requires approval of this exact report text, confirmed installation, passing required checks and independent Reviewer/Manager judgment. Phase remains Audit. If a material semantic, proof, trust or binding gap is found, return to Prove; perform no substantive repair in Audit.

//! Caller-visible candidate meaning for the STANDARD slice round trip.
//! The codec bridge is NOT yet established; see .system-proof/t1/handoff.md.
use vstd::prelude::*;
verus! {
/// Padded STANDARD length, in mathematical arithmetic.
pub open spec fn encoded_size(n: int) -> int { 4 * ((n + 2) / 3) }
/// Public conservative decode estimate, including nonmultiples of four.
pub open spec fn decode_capacity(m: int) -> int { 3 * ((m + 3) / 4) }
/// Arithmetic representability of the padded helper result, independent of buffers.
pub open spec fn helper_domain(n: int) -> bool {
    0 <= n <= usize::MAX && encoded_size(n) <= usize::MAX
}
/// Realizable byte-slice lengths (allocation availability is a separate condition).
pub open spec fn slice_length(n: int) -> bool { 0 <= n <= isize::MAX }
/// Domain for the exact public-helper-sized buffer recipe.
pub open spec fn roundtrip_domain(n: int) -> bool {
    slice_length(n) && helper_domain(n) && slice_length(encoded_size(n))
        && slice_length(decode_capacity(encoded_size(n)))
}
/// STANDARD's public alphabet, independent of implementation tables.
pub open spec fn alphabet(i: int) -> u8 {
    if i < 26 { (65 + i) as u8 }
    else if i < 52 { (97 + i - 26) as u8 }
    else if i < 62 { (48 + i - 52) as u8 }
    else if i == 62 { 43u8 } else { 47u8 }
}
/// Byte at an encoded position, with absent final source bytes zero extended.
pub open spec fn encoded_at(s: Seq<u8>, k: int) -> u8 {
    let b = (k / 4) * 3;
    let a = s[b] as int;
    let c = if b + 1 < s.len() { s[b + 1] as int } else { 0 };
    let d = if b + 2 < s.len() { s[b + 2] as int } else { 0 };
    if k % 4 == 0 { alphabet(a / 4) }
    else if k % 4 == 1 { alphabet((a % 4) * 16 + c / 16) }
    else if k % 4 == 2 {
        if b + 1 < s.len() { alphabet((c % 16) * 4 + d / 64) } else { 61u8 }
    } else {
        if b + 2 < s.len() { alphabet(d % 64) } else { 61u8 }
    }
}
/// Fully defined encoded bytes: no opaque relation between encoder and decoder.
pub open spec fn encoded(s: Seq<u8>) -> Seq<u8> {
    Seq::new(encoded_size(s.len() as int) as nat, |k: int| encoded_at(s, k))
}
/// Exact destination write plus frame, allowing arbitrary initial contents.
pub open spec fn writes(before: Seq<u8>, after: Seq<u8>, data: Seq<u8>) -> bool {
    data.len() <= before.len() && after.len() == before.len()
        && after.take(data.len() as int) == data
        && after.skip(data.len() as int) == before.skip(data.len() as int)
}
/// Preconditions at the byte-slice inner entry; buffers are already lawful slices.
pub open spec fn encode_pre(supplied: Seq<u8>, destination: Seq<u8>) -> bool {
    slice_length(supplied.len() as int) && slice_length(destination.len() as int)
        && helper_domain(supplied.len() as int)
        && encoded_size(supplied.len() as int) <= destination.len()
}
/// Decoder domain is encoder-produced strings, without implementation-success premises.
pub open spec fn decode_pre(original: Seq<u8>, supplied: Seq<u8>, destination: Seq<u8>) -> bool {
    roundtrip_domain(original.len() as int) && supplied == encoded(original)
        && slice_length(destination.len() as int)
        && decode_capacity(supplied.len() as int) <= destination.len()
}
/// `None` represents an API error for this observation; no error is permitted.
/// `supplied` denotes the bytes returned by the ONE actual as_ref invocation.
/// `before` is sampled at entry to the nested byte-slice inner body.
pub open spec fn encode_guarantee(
    supplied: Seq<u8>, before: Seq<u8>, after: Seq<u8>, reported: Option<usize>,
) -> bool {
    reported == Some(encoded_size(supplied.len() as int) as usize)
        && writes(before, after, encoded(supplied))
}
/// On strings produced by STANDARD, mandatory decode success and exact data.
/// `supplied` must equal encoded(original); caller passes exactly that prefix.
pub open spec fn decode_guarantee(
    original: Seq<u8>, supplied: Seq<u8>, before: Seq<u8>, after: Seq<u8>, reported: Option<usize>,
) -> bool {
    supplied == encoded(original) && reported == Some(original.len() as usize)
        && writes(before, after, original)
}
/// Independent buffer premises; no premise mentions implementation success.
pub open spec fn capacities(n: int, enc: int, dec: int) -> bool {
    roundtrip_domain(n) && slice_length(enc) && slice_length(dec)
        && encoded_size(n) <= enc && decode_capacity(encoded_size(n)) <= dec
}
}
roundtrip_caller.rs：A 的调用方base64-v4-A/proof/roundtrip_caller.rs · 25 行 · 1,262 字节

注释写明“conditional on D-ENC scalar and D-DEC”。

在 GitHub 打开原文件

//! Source-grounded checked exact-prefix consumer; conditional on D-ENC scalar and D-DEC.
//! Recipe follows encode_slice's truncate-to-reported-prefix doctest and
//! decode_slice's decoded_len_estimate sizing documentation.
use vstd::prelude::*;
use crate::{Engine as _, engine::general_purpose::STANDARD, encoded_len, decoded_len_estimate};
use crate::roundtrip_spec::*;
verus! {
/// Arbitrary input and initialized destinations; preserve their surplus bytes.
pub fn standard_roundtrip(s: &[u8], enc: &mut Vec<u8>, dec: &mut Vec<u8>) -> (used: usize)
    requires capacities(s@.len() as int, old(enc)@.len() as int, old(dec)@.len() as int),
    ensures
        used == s@.len(),
        writes(old(enc)@, final(enc)@, encoded(s@)),
        writes(old(dec)@, final(dec)@, s@),
{
    let encoded_capacity = encoded_len(s.len(), true).unwrap();
    let decoded_capacity = decoded_len_estimate(encoded_capacity);
    assert!(enc.len() >= encoded_capacity);
    assert!(dec.len() >= decoded_capacity);
    let written = STANDARD.encode_slice(s, enc.as_mut_slice()).unwrap();
    // Pass exactly the API-reported prefix, not its capacity or trailing bytes.
    let used = STANDARD.decode_slice(&enc.as_slice()[..written], dec.as_mut_slice()).unwrap();
    used
}
}

+//! Caller-visible candidate meaning for the STANDARD slice round trip.
+//! The codec bridge is NOT yet established; see .system-proof/t1/handoff.md.
+use vstd::prelude::*;
+verus! {
+/// Padded STANDARD length, in mathematical arithmetic.
+pub open spec fn encoded_size(n: int) -> int { 4 * ((n + 2) / 3) }
+/// Public conservative decode estimate, including nonmultiples of four.
+pub open spec fn decode_capacity(m: int) -> int { 3 * ((m + 3) / 4) }
+/// Arithmetic representability of the padded helper result, independent of buffers.
+pub open spec fn helper_domain(n: int) -> bool {
+    0 <= n <= usize::MAX && encoded_size(n) <= usize::MAX
+}
+/// Realizable byte-slice lengths (allocation availability is a separate condition).
+pub open spec fn slice_length(n: int) -> bool { 0 <= n <= isize::MAX }
+/// Domain for the exact public-helper-sized buffer recipe.
+pub open spec fn roundtrip_domain(n: int) -> bool {
+    slice_length(n) && helper_domain(n) && slice_length(encoded_size(n))
+        && slice_length(decode_capacity(encoded_size(n)))
+}
+/// STANDARD's public alphabet, independent of implementation tables.
+pub open spec fn alphabet(i: int) -> u8 {
+    if i < 26 { (65 + i) as u8 }
+    else if i < 52 { (97 + i - 26) as u8 }
+    else if i < 62 { (48 + i - 52) as u8 }
+    else if i == 62 { 43u8 } else { 47u8 }
+}
+/// Byte at an encoded position, with absent final source bytes zero extended.
+pub open spec fn encoded_at(s: Seq<u8>, k: int) -> u8 {
+    let b = (k / 4) * 3;
+    let a = s[b] as int;
+    let c = if b + 1 < s.len() { s[b + 1] as int } else { 0 };
+    let d = if b + 2 < s.len() { s[b + 2] as int } else { 0 };
+    if k % 4 == 0 { alphabet(a / 4) }
+    else if k % 4 == 1 { alphabet((a % 4) * 16 + c / 16) }
+    else if k % 4 == 2 {
+        if b + 1 < s.len() { alphabet((c % 16) * 4 + d / 64) } else { 61u8 }
+    } else {
+        if b + 2 < s.len() { alphabet(d % 64) } else { 61u8 }
+    }
+}
+/// Fully defined encoded bytes: no opaque relation between encoder and decoder.
+pub open spec fn encoded(s: Seq<u8>) -> Seq<u8> {
+    Seq::new(encoded_size(s.len() as int) as nat, |k: int| encoded_at(s, k))
+}
+/// Exact destination write plus frame, allowing arbitrary initial contents.
+pub open spec fn writes(before: Seq<u8>, after: Seq<u8>, data: Seq<u8>) -> bool {
+    data.len() <= before.len() && after.len() == before.len()
+        && after.take(data.len() as int) == data
+        && after.skip(data.len() as int) == before.skip(data.len() as int)
+}
+/// Preconditions at the byte-slice inner entry; buffers are already lawful slices.
+pub open spec fn encode_pre(supplied: Seq<u8>, destination: Seq<u8>) -> bool {
+    slice_length(supplied.len() as int) && slice_length(destination.len() as int)
+        && helper_domain(supplied.len() as int)
+        && encoded_size(supplied.len() as int) <= destination.len()
+}
+/// Decoder domain is encoder-produced strings, without implementation-success premises.
+pub open spec fn decode_pre(original: Seq<u8>, supplied: Seq<u8>, destination: Seq<u8>) -> bool {
+    roundtrip_domain(original.len() as int) && supplied == encoded(original)
+        && slice_length(destination.len() as int)
+        && decode_capacity(supplied.len() as int) <= destination.len()
+}
+/// `None` represents an API error for this observation; no error is permitted.
+/// `supplied` denotes the bytes returned by the ONE actual as_ref invocation.
+/// `before` is sampled at entry to the nested byte-slice inner body.
+pub open spec fn encode_guarantee(
+    supplied: Seq<u8>, before: Seq<u8>, after: Seq<u8>, reported: Option<usize>,
+) -> bool {
+    reported == Some(encoded_size(supplied.len() as int) as usize)
+        && writes(before, after, encoded(supplied))
+}
+/// On strings produced by STANDARD, mandatory decode success and exact data.
+/// `supplied` must equal encoded(original); caller passes exactly that prefix.
+pub open spec fn decode_guarantee(
+    original: Seq<u8>, supplied: Seq<u8>, before: Seq<u8>, after: Seq<u8>, reported: Option<usize>,
+) -> bool {
+    supplied == encoded(original) && reported == Some(original.len() as usize)
+        && writes(before, after, original)
+}
+/// Independent buffer premises; no premise mentions implementation success.
+pub open spec fn capacities(n: int, enc: int, dec: int) -> bool {
+    roundtrip_domain(n) && slice_length(enc) && slice_length(dec)
+        && encoded_size(n) <= enc && decode_capacity(encoded_size(n)) <= dec
+}
+}

A 组的任务原文base64-v4-A/request.txt · 50 行 · 5,885 字节

在 GitHub 打开原文件

Short goal:
Preserve arbitrary input bytes through the production Base64 encode_slice and decode_slice round trip, including exact reported prefixes, destination suffixes, generic input semantics, and the actual public callers.

Complete developer input (authoritative):
# Task: keep data unchanged through Base64 encoding and decoding

Use Verus to prove the properties below.

## What I need

I use this library to store or transmit binary data as text and recover the original data later. I want to verify that the real encoding and decoding APIs work together without losing, changing, duplicating or reordering bytes. This task is about lossless round-trip behavior, not a complete standards-compliance audit.

Use the registered production checkout at the fixed revision supplied with this task. The system-proof runtime provides that authoritative production checkout and a separate verification checkout of the same source. Treat every source path below as relative to the registered repository root; do not assume that either checkout is named `code/`. Read production to establish original behavior, make proof and verification adaptations in verification, and keep any authorized production fix and all production/verification drift explicit. Use the `GeneralPurpose` engine with `general_purpose::STANDARD`, disable default features, and enable only `std`: `--no-default-features --features std`. Do not use SIMD engines for this task.

## Top-level scope

| Entry point | What I use it for |
| --- | --- |
| `Engine::encode_slice` | Encode my input into a caller-provided buffer and report how many bytes were written |
| `Engine::decode_slice` | Decode exactly that reported encoded prefix into another caller-provided buffer |
| `encoded_len` and `decoded_len_estimate` | Size those buffers before making the two calls |

The public API is in `src/engine/mod.rs`. The selected implementation is in `src/engine/general_purpose/`; length/padding support and tables are in `src/encode.rs`, `src/decode.rs` and `src/alphabet.rs`. These supporting functions matter insofar as the real public calls depend on them; choose the internal breakdown yourself.

## Behavior I expect

1. For every input byte sequence within the supported length range, with buffers sized sufficiently using the public sizing helpers, encoding and subsequent decoding both succeed. The decoded length and every decoded byte equal the original input.
2. The caller passes only the encoded prefix reported by `encode_slice`, not unused capacity after it. Existing bytes in the output buffers must not influence the result.
3. Empty data, zero bytes, non-UTF-8 data and lengths on either side of the implementation's processing-block boundaries are all normal inputs. Do not limit the task to text strings or a few example lengths.
4. Input data is not modified. On success, bytes beyond the reported written prefix in each destination buffer remain unchanged.
5. State clearly which input lengths the platform and sizing helpers can represent. Within that range and the stated capacity conditions, reporting an error instead of recovering the data is not an acceptable substitute for success.

## When failure is permitted

No encoding or decoding error is permitted within the round-trip use described above: supported input length, sufficient caller-provided buffers, and decoding exactly the encoder's reported output. An error from an internal helper is not an additional permitted failure reason, and a panic is not a substitute for the required result.

Justify the supported length range from representation and capacity limits, not by defining it as whichever inputs the implementation accepts. Establish that the sizing helpers provide enough space in that range; do not assume their correctness to excuse a short output or a rejected encoding. Arbitrary external encoded strings, insufficient buffers and unrepresentable lengths remain outside this task, not exceptions that may be applied to supported calls.

## What is not part of this request

I am not asking for interoperability with another Base64 implementation, all RFC rules, arbitrary malformed-string behavior, exact error messages/offsets, insufficient-output behavior, streaming readers/writers, other alphabets/configurations, allocation internals or timing guarantees.

The decoder must work on every encoded string that this selected encoder produces for the inputs above. This does not require a promise about every other string the decoder could receive.

## What to return

Tell me which requested behaviors you established for these actual APIs, including the absence of unjustified errors in the supported round-trip, any counterexample or bug, the code and configuration covered, and remaining limitations or environmental assumptions. Keep the original revision identifiable. If code changes are needed, separate findings about the original from the changed version; do not replace the codec with another algorithm and report that as verification of this source.

---
How to run this here: use the system_proof skill pack (read its SKILL.md first; each role then reads roles/<role>.md from it). Production worktree (authoritative source, read-only except as the pack allows): /fastdata/v-boxiuli/noah/exp/ts/production. Verification worktree, where all proof work happens: /fastdata/v-boxiuli/noah/exp/ts/verification. The Verus toolchain and the system-proof tools (verus-run, proof-map, specdet, boundary-scan, drift-check, verus-gap, ...) are on PATH. Each role's live context per phase: /fastdata/v-boxiuli/noah/exp/ts/verification/.system-proof/context/<role>.<phase>.json. Target hints: Engine::encode_slice,Engine::decode_slice,GeneralPurpose::encode_slice,GeneralPurpose::decode_slice,encoded_len,decoded_len_estimate. The tools/, home/, codex/ and logs/ folders here are infrastructure, not part of the task.

过程与不公平之处

  • 版本不同：A 跑时技能包还没有恢复审计重放检查，所以 A 自己没发现结果过不了原地审计。按同一个标准，A 也会被拦下，然后不得不接着做。
  • C 有人工推动：C 一度要自己宣布失败，是我发消息让它继续的；改写路线也是我批准的。批准前 ground 阶段花了 5 小时 21 分钟，大部分耗在 Verus 编译器内部；批准后 8 分钟过 ground，prove 用 16 分钟，audit 用 18 分钟。
  • C 一边跑一边升级 Argus：C 一开始就用 task worktree；跑的过程中又换了 5 次 Argus 版本，逐步加上四个角色边做边协作和持久 Reviewer，每次重启都从原处接着做。13:37–14:00 因“同一任务上两次‘先看’同时进行”的 bug 停过一次，已修好。C 的协作次数：回合中途写信 159 次，请 Reviewer “先看”130 次，Planner 实时收到交接抄送 216 次。
  • 已知小毛病：C 完成时 Manager 发了 3 条几乎一样的完工消息；T74、T76 的原始临时审查目录没保留下来（报告已注明）；差异清单的解析器只解析了一部分（METRIC-08）。
  • 正在跑：D 组用现在的 Argus 主线，从头跑同一题，任务原文里一开始就允许“不改行为的改写”，全程不人工介入。结果出来后另行补充。

复现 C 组的审计重放

在原机器上运行，工具链在验证 checkout 的 .tools/ 下。这正是插件审计时跑的命令：

C=/fastdata/v-boxiuli/noah/exp/tsC
T=$C/verification/.tools
export RUSTUP_HOME=$T/rustup CARGO_HOME=$T/cargo
export SYSTEM_PROOF_VERUS_HOME=$T/system-proof-agent/vertical/argus_system_proof/tools/verus-validation/toolchain/bin
export VERUS_Z3_PATH=$SYSTEM_PROOF_VERUS_HOME/z3
export PATH=$T/system-proof-agent/.venv/bin:$SYSTEM_PROOF_VERUS_HOME:$CARGO_HOME/bin:$PATH
verus-run verify $C/verification --preset standard   # 期望：172 verified, 0 errors
